" tests which are the main new elements to prove the
L_{2}
posterior contraction (see the proof of Theorem
3.1
). These tests are based on renewal properties of the Hawkes process coming from
[
58
]
and
[
17
]
. In particular, the tests statistics are built on a restricted window
A_{2}(T)\subset[0,T]
, introduced in
[
58
]
, on which the process can be decomposed as a sum of i.i.d. point processes.

Before presenting the tests, we first recall some renewal properties of the Hawkes process and make some definitions. The sequence of random times
(\tau_{n})_{n\geq 0}
(called renewal times) is defined by

\tau_{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\mbox{ if }n=0\\
\inf\big\{t>\tau_{n-1}:N([t-A,t[\neq 0,N(]t-A,t])=0\big\}&\mbox{ if }n\geq 1\end{array}\right..

By Lemma 5.1 of
[
58
]
, the variables
(\tau_{n})_{n}
are stopping times and the point processes
(N_{|[\tau_{n},\tau_{n+1}[})_{n\geq 1}
are i.i.d.. Let
\bar{\tau}:=\mathbb{E}_{0}[\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}]
. Denote by
U^{(1)}_{n}
and
U^{(2)}_{n}
the the two first events after
\tau_{n}
. By Lemma 5.1 of
[
58
]
, there exists
v>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
,
\mathbb{E}[e^{(U^{(1)}_{n}-\tau_{n})v}]<+\infty
. For
T>0
, let
n_{T}:=\max\{n\geq 0,\tau_{n}\leq T\}
and set

\displaystyle\Omega_{\tau,T}:=\Big\{n_{T}-1\in\Big[\frac{T}{2\bar{\tau}},\frac{2T}{\bar{\tau}}\Big]\hskip 5.69046pt\text{and}\hskip 5.69046ptU^{(1)}_{n}-\tau_{n}\geq 2v^{-1}\log(T),\hskip 2.84544pt1\leq n\leq n_{T}-1\Big\}.

(C.2)

With Lemma 5.1 and Lemma 5.4 of
[
58
]
, we have that
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{\tau,T})\rightarrow 0
. Next, set
\chi_{n}:=\min(U^{(2)}_{n},\tau_{n+1})
; it is a stopping time as the minimum of two stopping times. The announced restricted window
A_{2}(T)
is defined by

\displaystyle A_{2}(T):=\bigcup_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}[\tau_{n},\chi_{n}].

We introduce the stochastic distance
d_{T}
:

\displaystyle d_{T}^{2}(f,f^{\prime}):=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{A_{2}(T)}(t)\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{\prime}_{k})^{2}dt=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\chi_{n}}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{\prime}_{k})^{2}dt.

Note that similarly to the equation (29) from
[
58
]
, we have for some universal
C>0

\displaystyle d_{T}^{2}(f,f^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{C}{T}\sum_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}(A+1+\tau_{n+1}-\tau_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{C}{T}\big((n_{T}-1)(A+1)+\tau_{n_{T}}-\tau_{1}\big).

and thus on the event
\Omega_{\tau,T}
we have for some universal
\eta>0
,

\displaystyle d_{T}^{2}(f,f^{\prime})\leq\eta\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}.

(C.3)

Now, as in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we define "sieves"
\mathcal{F}_{T}
depending on
\varphi
. When
\varphi
is the identity function, we set
\mathcal{F}_{T}=\mathcal{W}_{T}^{1}
and we divide it into into
slices
\mathcal{F}_{T}(r):=\big\{f\in\mathcal{F}_{T},\|f-f^{0}\|_{2}\in[r\varepsilon_{T},(r+1)\varepsilon_{T}[\big\}
,
r\geq 1
. It is shown in the proof of Theorem
3.1
that it is enough to take the slices with
r\leq\bar{r}_{T}:=\lceil 2DC_{1}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}\rceil
(
D
and
C_{1}
being a universal positive constants) to cover
\mathcal{F}_{T}
. When
\varphi
is not the identity function, we define
\mathcal{F}_{T}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{T}=\big\{f\in\mathcal{W}^{1}_{T},\|\mathbf{h}\|_{\infty}\leq r_{T}\big\}\cap\big\{f:\|\mathbf{h}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{2}\geq\log(T)r_{T}\varepsilon_{T}\hskip 4.26773pt\text{or}\hskip 4.26773pt\|\mathbf{h}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq G\big\}.

The slices
(\mathcal{F}_{T}(r))_{r\geq 1}
are then defined in the same way and it is enough to take the slices with
r\leq\bar{r}_{T}:=\lceil 2Ar_{T}\rceil
to cover
\mathcal{F}_{T}
. Next, whatever
\varphi
, let
(f^{r,i})_{i\leq\mathcal{N}_{r}}
be the centering points of a covering of
\mathcal{F}_{T}(r)
in terms of metric
\|.\|_{2}
and with radius
\varepsilon_{T}(r+1)\Delta/(4\eta)
for some
\Delta>0
. With the entropy computations done in the proof of proposition
3.1
, we have that
\mathcal{N}_{r}\leq e^{x_{0}^{\prime}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}}
for some
x_{0}^{\prime}>0
. Then, we define the sequence of events
(\Xi_{T}(r))_{r\geq 1}
by

\displaystyle\Xi_{T}(r)=\big\{d_{T}^{2}(f^{0},f^{r,i})\geq\Delta\|f^{0}-f^{r,i}\|_{2}^{2},\hskip 2.84544pt\forall i\leq\mathcal{N}_{r}\big\},\quad\displaystyle\Xi_{T}:=\bigcap_{r=1}^{\bar{r}_{T}}\Xi_{T}(r).

and we have the following lemma.

Lemma C.1
.

Under assumptions of Theorem
3.1
, there exists
\Delta>0
such that
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Xi_{T}^{c})=o(1)
.

This lemma is proved at the end of this section. The announced
L_{2}
tests are built in the following lemma with the desired exponential decay for the type II error on the event
\Xi_{T}
. As in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we set
\underline{r}_{T}=\lfloor\log(T)\rfloor
.

Lemma C.2
.

Under assumptions of Theorem
3.1
, let
\underline{r}_{T}\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{T}
,
i\leq\mathcal{N}_{r}
and
f^{r,i}
as previously. Let also
\Delta>0
as in Lemma
C.1
. Define the test
\phi_{f^{r,i}}
by

\displaystyle\phi_{f^{r,i}}=\mathds{1}\Big\{\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{A_{2}(T)}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})\big(dN_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt\big)\geq\Delta T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}/4\Big\}.

Then, whatever the link function
\varphi
, there exists
T_{0}>0
,
c>0
, both independent of
f^{r,i}
, such that for
T\geq T_{0}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{\tau,T}}\phi_{f^{r,i}}]\lesssim e^{-cT\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r},

\displaystyle\underset{f:\|f-f^{r,i}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\Delta\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}}{4\eta}}{\sup}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\mathbb{E}_{f}\big[\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Xi_{T}(r)}(1-\phi_{f^{r,i}})\big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\big]\big]\lesssim e^{-cT\varepsilon_{T}^{2}\log(T)}.

We first bound the type I error.
For
1\leq n\leq n_{T}-1
, we set

\displaystyle W_{n}^{k}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\chi_{n}}\tilde{\lambda}^{k}_{t}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})(dN_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt)

\displaystyle=(\nu^{r,i}_{k}-\nu^{0}_{k})+\mathds{1}_{\{\tau_{n+1}>U^{(2)}_{n},N^{k}(\{U^{(2)}_{n}\})=1\}}\Big((\nu^{1}_{k}-\nu^{0}_{k})+\sum_{l=1}^{K}\mathds{1}_{\{N^{l}(\{U^{(1)}_{n}\})=1\}}(h^{r,i}_{l,k}-h^{0}_{l,k})(U^{(2)}_{n}-U^{(1)}_{n})\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\int_{{\tau_{n}}}^{\chi_{n}}\tilde{\lambda}^{k}_{t}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt.

The variables
(\sum_{k=1}^{K}W_{n}^{k})_{n\geq 1}
are i.i.d. and centered by Lemma
C.4
. The process
(\sum_{n=1}^{\bar{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}W_{n}^{k})_{\bar{n}\geq 1}
is a centered martingale with respect to the filtration generated by the
(W_{n}^{1},...,W_{n}^{K})_{n\geq 1}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=1\}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Big\{\sum_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}W_{n}^{k}\geq T\Delta\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Big/4\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Big\{\underset{\bar{n}\leq 2T/\bar{\tau}}{\max}\sum_{n=1}^{\bar{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}W_{n}^{k}\geq T\Delta\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}/4\Big\}\bigg).

To obtain the exponential decay of the type I error, we apply the Bernstein inequality for martingales of
[
22
]
to the right hand side of last inequality. To do so, we have to bound on
\Omega_{\tau,T}
the random variables
(\sum_{k=1}^{K}W_{n}^{k})_{n}
and also, since these variables are i.i.d. and centered, to upper bound the variance of their sum which is
\bar{n}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}W_{1}^{k}\big)^{2}\big]
. First, with similar computations as in equation (29) of
[
58
]
, for
k\in[K]
we have that

\displaystyle|W_{1}^{k}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\chi_{1}}|\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})|\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt

(C.4)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}+2(A+1+U^{(1)}_{1}-\tau_{1})\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}.

In particular, we have on the event
\Omega_{\tau,T}
, for all
k\in[K]
and
n\leq n_{T}-1
,
\big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}W_{n}^{k}\big|\lesssim\log(T)\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}
. Secondly, for the variance, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[(W_{1}^{k})^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu^{1}_{k}-\nu^{0}_{k})^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[(h^{r,i}_{l,k}-h^{0}_{l,k})^{2}(U^{(2)}_{1}-U^{(1)}_{1})\mathds{1}_{\{\tau_{2}>U^{(2)}_{1},N^{l}(\{U^{(1)}_{1}\})=1,N^{k}(\{U^{(2)}_{1}\})=1\}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\Big(\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\chi_{n}}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt\Big)^{2}\bigg].

With equation (30) of
[
58
]
, we further have

\displaystyle E_{0}\bigg[\Big(\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\chi_{n}}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt\Big)^{2}\bigg]\lesssim\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

Then, by the cluster representation of the Hawkes process (see
[
32
]
),
U^{(2)}_{1}
is either an immigrant point and, conditionally on the event "
U^{(2)}_{1}
is marked by
k
",
U^{(2)}_{1}-U^{(1)}_{1}
follows an exponential distribution with parameter
\nu^{0}_{k}
; or
U^{(2)}_{1}
is an offspring of
U^{(1)}_{1}
and conditionally and on the event "
\tau_{2}>U^{(2)}_{1}
,
U^{(1)}_{1}
is marked by
l
and
U^{(2)}_{1}
is marked by
k
",
U^{(2)}_{1}-U^{(1)}_{1}
follows a distribution with a density proportional to
x\mapsto h^{0}_{l,k}(x)e^{-\int_{0}^{x}h_{l,k}^{0}(u)du}
. Whence, for some finite
c>0
we have

\sum_{l=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[(h^{r,i}_{l,k}-h^{0}_{l,k})^{2}(U^{(2)}_{j}-U^{(1)}_{j})\mathds{1}_{\{\tau_{2}>U^{(2)}_{1},N^{l}(\{U^{(1)}_{1}\})=1,N^{k}(\{U^{(2)}_{1}\})=1\}}\Big]\leq c\|f^{0}\|_{1}\|\mathbf{h}^{r,i}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{2}^{2},

and therefore
\mathbb{E}_{0}[(W_{1}^{k})^{2}]\leq(1+c\|f^{0}\|_{1}+\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2})\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}
. We can now apply Theorem 3.3 of
[
22
]
which gives that for some
c^{\prime}>0
that depends only on
K
and
\bar{\tau}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=1\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{-c^{\prime}\Delta^{2}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{(1+c\|f^{0}\|_{1}+\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2})+\Delta\log(T)\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

If
\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq(1+c\|f^{0}\|_{1}+\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2})/(\Delta\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\log(T))
, as
l\geq\log(T)
, then we have for
T
large enough

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=1\}\big)\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta^{2}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r^{2}}{2(1+c\|f^{0}\|_{1}+\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2})}\bigg)\leq\exp\bigg(-\Delta^{2}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r\bigg).

Now, we consider that
\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}\geq(1+c\|f^{0}\|_{1}+\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2})/(\Delta\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\log(T))
(and in particular for
T
large enough,
\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}\geq\varepsilon_{T}
). In this case we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=1\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{-c^{\prime}\Delta T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\log(T)\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

When
\varphi
is the identity function, with Lemma
C.3
we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=1\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{4D\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta T\varepsilon_{T}r}{4D\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r}{4D\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}}\bigg)\leq 2\exp\big(-c^{\prime}\Delta T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r\big),

for
T
large enough since
\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}\rightarrow 0
. When
\varphi
is not the identity function, we recall that by definition of
\mathcal{F}_{T}(r)
, we have
\|\mathbf{h}^{r,i}\|_{\infty}\leq r_{T}
and if
\|\mathbf{h}^{r,i}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{2}\leq\log(T)r_{T}\varepsilon_{T}
then
\|\mathbf{h}^{r,i}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq G
. So, if
l\geq\log(T)r_{T}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=1\}\big)\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r^{2}}{2\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\log(T)r_{T}}\bigg)\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta^{2}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r}{2\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}}\bigg),

and if
\lfloor\log(T)\rfloor\leq r\leq\log(T)r_{T}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=1\}\big)\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r^{2}}{2G\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}\log(T)}\Big)\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{c^{\prime}\Delta^{2}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}r}{2G\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

It terminates the proof for the type I error.

For the type II error, let
f\in\mathcal{F}_{T}(r)
be such that
\|f^{r,i}-f\|_{2}\leq\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}\Delta/(4\eta)
. Let
\bar{\Omega}_{T}=\Xi_{T}(r)\cap\Omega_{\tau,T}
(with
\Xi_{T}(r)
and
\Omega_{\tau,T}
defined at the start of section
C.2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{f}\big(\bar{\Omega}_{T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=0\}\big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{f}\bigg(\bar{\Omega}_{T}\cap\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{A_{2}(T)}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})(dN_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt)\leq\frac{\Delta}{4}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\Big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\bigg).

Recall that
\mathbb{P}_{f}
is the distribution of a stationary ReLu Hawkes process whose intensity is given by
\lambda_{t}(f_{k})=\tilde{\lambda}_{t}(f_{k})_{+}
. Recall also that by definition of
\mathcal{F}_{R}
we have for all
k\in[K]
,
\nu_{k}-\underset{l\in[K]}{\max}\|h^{-}_{l,k}\|_{\infty}>0
. Let
n\geq 1
and
t\in[\tau_{n},\chi_{n}]
. Assume without loss of generality that
U^{(1)}_{N}
is marked by
l
, then we obtain:

\displaystyle\lambda^{k}_{t}(f_{k})=\Big(\nu_{k}+\mathds{1}_{t>U^{(1)}_{n}}h_{l,k}(t-U^{(1)}_{n})\Big)_{+}=\nu_{k}+\mathds{1}_{t>U^{(1)}_{n}}h_{l,k}(t-U^{(1)}_{n}=\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k})

On the event
\Xi_{T}(r)
, with the previous remark, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and with the inequality (
C.3
) we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta}{4}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{A_{2}(T)}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})(\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k}))dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta}{4}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{A_{2}(T)}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta}{4}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}-Td_{T}^{2}(f^{r,i},f^{0})-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{A_{2}(T)}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{r,i}_{k})dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta}{4}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}-T\Delta\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}+T\sqrt{d_{T}^{2}(f^{r,i},f^{0})d_{T}^{2}(f^{r,i},f)}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{3\Delta}{4}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}+T\eta\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}\|f-f^{r,i}\|_{2}\leq-\frac{\Delta}{2}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2},

as soon as
\|f-f^{r,i}\|_{2}\leq\Delta\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}/(4\eta)
.
Let

\displaystyle Y^{k}_{n}:=-\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\chi_{n}}\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})\big(dN_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})dt\big).

Then the above inequality, together with the inclusion

\Omega_{\tau,T}\subset\big\{U^{(1)}_{n}-\tau_{n}\geq 2v^{-1}\log(T),\hskip 2.84544pt1\leq n\leq 2T/\bar{\tau}\big\},

leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{f}\big(\bar{\Omega}_{T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=0\}\big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{f}\bigg(\bar{\Omega}_{T}\cap\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{A_{2}(T)}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k})(dN_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})dt)\leq-\frac{\Delta}{2}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\Big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{f}\bigg(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}Y^{k}_{n}\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\Big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}_{f}\bigg(\big\{U^{(1)}_{n}-\tau_{n}\geq 2v^{-1}\log(T),\hskip 2.84544pt1\leq n\leq 2T/\bar{\tau}\big\}\cap\Big\{\underset{\bar{n}\leq 2T/\bar{\tau}}{\max}\sum_{n=1}^{\bar{n}}Y^{k}_{n}\geq\frac{\Delta}{2K}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\Big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}_{f}\bigg(\big\{U^{(1)}_{n}-\tau_{n}\geq 2v^{-1}\log(T),\hskip 2.84544pt1\leq n\leq 2T/\bar{\tau}\big\}\cap\Big\{\underset{\bar{n}\leq 2T/\bar{\tau}}{\max}\sum_{n=1}^{\bar{n}}Y^{k}_{n}\geq\frac{\Delta}{2K}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\bigg),

and for the last equality we have used that for
n\geq 1
, the variables
Y_{n}^{k}
and
U^{(1)}_{n}-\tau_{n}
are independent of
\mathcal{G}_{0-}
. Now, we can proceed for the
(Y^{k}_{n})_{n}
under
\mathbb{P}_{f}
exactly as for the
(W^{k}_{n})_{n}
under
\mathbb{P}_{0}
and with Theorem 3.3 of
[
22
]
we find that some
c^{\prime\prime}
that depends only on
K
and
\bar{\tau}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{f}\big(\bar{\Omega}_{T}\cap\{\phi_{f^{r,i}}=0\}\big|\mathcal{G}_{0-}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{-c^{\prime\prime}\Delta^{2}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{(1+c\|f\|_{1}+\|f\|_{\infty}^{2})+\Delta\log(T)\|f\|_{\infty}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{-c^{\prime\prime}\Delta^{2}T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{(1+c\|f\|_{1}+2\|f-f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+2\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2})+\Delta\log(T)(\|f-f^{0}\|_{\infty}+\|f^{0}\|_{\infty})\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

As
\|f-f^{0}\|_{1}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{T}
by definition of
\mathcal{F}_{T}(r)
, we have for
T
large enough
c\|f\|_{1}\leq 2c\|f^{0}\|_{1}
. Then, we distinguish cases and follow the same steps as for type I error. In particular, when
\varphi
is not the identity function, we bound
\|f-f^{0}\|_{\infty}
and
\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}
by
G
if
r\leq\log(T)r_{T}
and by
r_{T}
otherwise, and we obtain the result in this case. When
\varphi
is the identity function, we use Lemma
C.3
to upper bound
\|f-f^{0}\|_{\infty}
and
\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{\infty}
in terms of their
L_{2}
norms and then, using that
\|f-f^{r,i}\|_{2}\leq\Delta\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}/(4\eta)
and that
\varepsilon^{2}_{T}J_{T}\log(T)\rightarrow 0
, we obtain also the result. It concludes the proof for the type II error.
∎

The two following lemmas are technical lemmas used in the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Lemma C.3
.

Let
f=(\nu,\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))
with
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(j)^{K^{2}}
for some
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and such that
\|\nu-\nu^{0}\|_{1}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{T}
. There exists some
D>0
independent of
f
such that for
T
large enough,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq D\sqrt{J_{T}}\big(\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}+\varepsilon_{T}\big)\hskip 5.69046pt,\hskip 8.5359pt\|f-f^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq D\sqrt{J_{T}}\big(\|f-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{2}+\varepsilon_{T}\big).

Since for all
j
,
\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(j)\subset\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(Rj)
(see section
3.1
), then for all
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(j)^{K^{2}}
for some
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
, we have
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(mJ_{T})^{K^{2}}
, for some
m\in[R]
. Then, by assumption
(P1)
, there exists
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{*}\in\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(mJ_{T})^{K^{2}}
such that for
T
large enough
\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{*}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{T}
. With in addition (
3.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{*}\|_{\infty}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{*}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{m}\sqrt{J_{T}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{*}\|_{2}+\varepsilon_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{m}\sqrt{J_{T}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}+\sqrt{m}\sqrt{J_{T}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{*}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{0}\|_{2}+\varepsilon_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{m}\sqrt{J_{T}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}+(\sqrt{Am}\sqrt{J_{T}}+1)\varepsilon_{T}.

Thus, there exists
\underline{D}>0
independent of
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}
such that
\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\underline{D}\sqrt{J_{T}}\big(\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}+\varepsilon_{T}\big)
. Since
\varphi
is Lipschitz and since
\|\nu-\nu^{0}\|_{1}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{T}
, there exists
D\geq\underline{D}
such that
\|f-f^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq D\sqrt{J_{T}}\big(\|f-f^{0}\|_{2}+\varepsilon_{T}\big)
.
∎

Lemma C.4
.

The variables
(W_{n}^{k})_{k\in[K],n\geq 1}
introduced in the proof of Lemma
C.2
are centered in expectation.

. The variables
(W_{n}^{k})_{n\geq 1}
are i.i.d. so it is enough to show that
\mathbb{E}_{0}[W_{1}^{k}]=0
. To do so, let
\mathcal{N}
be a Hawkes process with parameters
f^{0}
and born in
0
, that is a
[K]
-marked process with
\mathcal{N}(]-\infty,0[)=0
and for
t\geq 0
the intensity is given by,

\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0},\mathcal{N})=\nu^{0}_{k}+\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{t-}h_{l,k}^{0}(t-u)d\mathcal{N}^{l}_{u}.

(C.5)

For
a\geq 0
, we introduce

\displaystyle\bar{W}_{a}^{k}(\mathcal{N})=\int_{0}^{a}\tilde{\lambda}^{k}_{t}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k},\mathcal{N})(d\mathcal{N}_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},\mathcal{N})dt),

where
\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k},\mathcal{N})
is given by (
C.5
) but with
f^{0}
replace by
f^{r,i}-f^{0}
.
(\bar{W}_{a}^{k}(\mathcal{N}))_{a\geq 0}
is a cadlag martingale with respect to the filtration generated by
\mathcal{N}
. Moreover, by the renewal property,
W_{1}^{k}
is distributed as
W_{\chi^{\prime}}^{k}(\mathcal{N})
where
\chi^{\prime}
is the stopping time equal to the minimum between the time of the second point of
M
and the first renewal time of
\mathcal{N}
. So it is equivalent to prove that
\mathbb{E}[W_{\chi^{\prime}}^{k}(\mathcal{N})]=0
. By the inequality (
C.4
),
\mathbb{E}[|W_{\chi^{\prime}}^{k}(\mathcal{N})|]<+\infty
. Moreover, in the same way as
\chi_{1}
has exponential moments by Lemma 5.2 of
[
58
]
,
\chi^{\prime}
has exponential moments in the sense that there exists
\delta>0
such that
\mathbb{E}[e^{\chi^{\prime}\delta}]<+\infty
. As a consequence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\bar{W}_{a}^{k}(\mathcal{N})|\mathds{1}_{\chi^{\prime}>a}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty}(1+a\|f^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty})\mathbb{P}(\chi^{\prime}>a)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty}(1+a\|f^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty})e^{-a\delta}\xrightarrow[a\rightarrow+\infty]{}0.

Hence, with these remarks we can apply the martingale optional sampling Theorem (see Theorem 2.13 of
[
25
]
for instance) and we have that
\mathbb{E}[W_{\chi^{\prime}}^{k}(\mathcal{N})]=E[W_{0}^{k}(\mathcal{N})]=0
. It concludes the proof of Lemma
C.4
.
∎

Finally, we prove Lemma
C.1
on the stochastic distance
d_{T}
.

Recall
\Omega_{\tau,T}
defined in (
C.2
). We recall that for some
x_{0}^{\prime}>0
,
\mathcal{N}_{r}\leq\exp(x_{0}^{\prime}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2})
(see the proof of Theorem
3.1
). First, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Xi_{T}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Xi_{T}^{c})+P_{0}(\Omega_{\tau,T}^{c})\leq\sum_{r=\underline{r}_{T}}^{\bar{r}_{T}}\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Xi_{T}(r)^{c})+o(1)

(C.6)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(x_{0}^{\prime}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2})\sum_{r=\underline{r}_{T}}^{\bar{r}_{T}}\max_{i\leq\mathcal{N}_{r}}\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\{d_{T}^{2}(f^{0},f^{r,i}\big)\leq\Delta\|f^{0}-f^{r,i}\|_{2}\}\Big)+o(1).

For
\underline{r}_{T}\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{T}
,
i\leq\mathcal{N}_{r}
,
k\in[K]
and
1\leq n\leq n_{T}-1
, we set

\displaystyle X_{n}^{k}=\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\chi_{n}}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k}-f^{r,i}_{k})^{2}dt,

with the variables
\tau_{n}
and
\chi_{n}
that are defined at the start of section
C.2
. Let
\Delta>0
that will be fixed later, the variables
(X_{n}^{1},....,X_{n}^{K})_{n}
are i.i.d. and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\big\{d_{T}^{2}(f^{0},f^{r,i})\leq\Delta\|f^{0}-f^{r,i_{*}}\|_{2}\}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Big\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}X_{n}^{k}\leq\Delta\|f^{0}-f^{r,i}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Big\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}X_{n}^{k}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[X_{n}^{k}]\leq\Delta\|f^{0}-f^{r,i_{*}}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{n_{T}-1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}[X_{1}^{k}]\Big\}\bigg).

Now, we lower bound
\mathbb{E}_{0}[X^{k}_{1}]
. For this, let
\mathbb{Q}
be the
K
-marked process consisting in
K
independent component each being a Poisson process on
[0;+\infty[
with constant intensity equal to
1
. For each
l\in[K]
set

\displaystyle\Omega_{l}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\underset{l^{\prime}\neq l}{\max}N^{l^{\prime}}([\tau_{1},\tau_{2}[)=0,N^{l}([\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+x[)=0,N^{l}([\tau_{1}+x,\tau_{1}+x+A])=1,

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274ptN^{l}([\tau_{1}+x+A,\tau_{2}[)=0\Big\},

with
x\in]0,A[
.
\mathbb{Q}(\Omega_{l})>0
, see page 46 of the supplementary of
[
58
]
. Mimicking the proof of lemma A.4 of
[
58
]
, we find that for all
k\in[K]
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[X_{1}^{k}]

\displaystyle\geq C_{k}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\bigg[\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{l}}\int_{U_{1}^{(1)}}^{U_{1}^{(1)}+A}\Big(\nu_{k}^{r,i}-\nu_{0}^{k}+(h_{l,k}^{r,i}-h^{0}_{l,k})(t-U_{1}^{(1)})\Big)^{2}dt\bigg]

\displaystyle=(\nu_{k}^{r,i}-\nu_{0}^{k})^{2}AC_{k}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\mathbb{Q}(\Omega_{l})+C_{k}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\bigg[\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{l}}\int_{U_{1}^{(1)}}^{U_{1}^{(1)}+A}(h_{l,k}^{r,i}-h^{0}_{l,k})^{2}(t-U_{1}^{(1)})dt\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+2(\nu_{k}^{r,i}-\nu_{0}^{k})C_{k}\sum_{l=1}^{K}E_{\mathbb{Q}}\bigg[\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{l}}\int_{U_{1}^{(1)}}^{U_{1}^{(1)}+A}(h_{l,k}^{r,i}-h^{0}_{l,k})(t-U_{1}^{(1)})dt\bigg].

Then, with the same computations as at the end of the page 46 of the supplementary of
[
58
]
, we find that there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[X_{1}^{k}]\gtrsim(\nu_{k}^{r,i}-\nu_{0}^{k})^{2}+\|\mathbf{h}_{k}^{r,i}-\mathbf{h}^{0}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}-c|\nu_{k}^{r,i}-\nu_{0}^{k}|\times\|\mathbf{h}_{k}^{r,i}-\mathbf{h}^{0}_{k}\|_{1}.

Moreover, since
\mathcal{F}_{T}(r)\subset\mathcal{W}^{1}_{T}
, we have
\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}\geq r\varepsilon_{T}
,
\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{1}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{T}
and thus

\displaystyle|\nu_{k}^{r,i}-\nu_{0}^{k}|\times\|\mathbf{h}_{l}^{r,i}-\mathbf{h}^{0}_{k}\|_{1}\leq C_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{r^{2}}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{\lfloor\log(T)\rfloor^{2}}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2},

where for the last inequality we have used that
r\geq\lfloor\log(T)\rfloor
. So, for some universal
d>0
and
T
large enough

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}[X_{1}^{k}]\geq d\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

(C.7)

Letting
\Delta=d/\bar{\tau}
and using the definition of
\Omega_{\tau,T}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\big\{d_{T}^{2}(f^{0},f^{r,i})\leq\Delta\|f^{0}-f^{r,i}\|_{2}\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Big\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{n=1}^{n_{T}-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}X_{n}^{k}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[X_{n}^{k}]\leq-\frac{\Delta}{2}\|f^{0}-f^{r,i}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\Big\{\underset{\bar{n}\leq 2T/\bar{\tau}}{\max}\sum_{n=1}^{\bar{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}-X_{n}^{k}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[-X_{n}^{k}]\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}T\|f^{0}-f^{r,i}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\bigg).

Since the variables
(\sum_{k=1}^{K}-X_{n}^{k}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[-X_{n}^{k}])_{n}
are i.i.d. and centered, the process
(S_{\bar{n}})_{\bar{n}\geq 0}
defined for
\bar{n}\geq 1
by
S_{\bar{n}}:=\sum_{n=1}^{\bar{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}-X_{n}^{k}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[-X_{n}^{k}]
and
S_{0}=0
, is a martingale with respect to its natural filtration. By Theorem 3.3 of
[
22
]
, we have for any
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\underset{\bar{n}\leq\lceil 2T/\bar{\tau}\rceil}{\max}|S_{\bar{n}}|>x,\hskip 2.84544pt\hat{v}_{\lceil 2T/\bar{\tau}\rceil}\leq v\hskip 2.84544pt\text{and}\hskip 2.84544pt\hat{b}_{\lceil 2T/\bar{\tau}\rceil}\leq b\bigg)\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{x^{2}}{v+xb/3}\Big),

with

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{\bar{n}}:=\sum_{n=1}^{\bar{n}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}X_{n}^{k}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[X_{n}^{k}]\Big)^{2}\Big]\hskip 5.69046pt\text{and}\hskip 5.69046pt\hat{b}_{\bar{n}}:=\underset{n\leq\bar{n}}{\max}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}-X_{n}^{k}-\mathbb{E}_{0}[-X_{n}^{k}]\Big|.

Similarly to the equation (29) of
[
58
]
, we have
0\leq X_{n}^{k}\lesssim(A+1+U^{(1)}_{n}-\tau_{n})\|f^{r,i}_{k}-f^{0}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}
. As a consequence, on the event
\Omega_{\tau,T}
,
\hat{b}_{{\lceil 2T/\bar{\tau}\rceil}}\lesssim\log(T)\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{2}
. Moreover,
\hat{v}_{\bar{n}}\lesssim\bar{n}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{4}
. Therefore, we obtain that for some
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\tau,T}\cap\big\{d_{T}^{2}(f^{0},f^{r,i})\leq\Delta\|f^{0}-f^{r,i}\|_{2}\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{C\Delta^{2}T^{2}\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{4}}{T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{4}+\Delta\log(T)T\|f^{r,i}-f^{0}\|_{2}^{4}}\bigg)\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{C\Delta T}{2\log(T)}\bigg),

for
T
large enough. To conclude, we come back to (
C.6
) and with the previous bound we now have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Xi_{T}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{r}_{T}\exp\bigg(T\Big(x_{0}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}-\frac{C\Delta}{2\log(T)}\Big)\bigg)+o(1)\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{}0,

which terminates the proof of Lemma
C.1
.
∎

C.3
Lemmas related to the BvM property

In this section, we state and prove lemmas related to the BvM property. We first prove the following Lemma
C.5
on the control of the bias term in Theorem
3.2
and Corollary
3.1
. Then, we also prove Lemma
C.6
which concerns the contraction of the
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}
on
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}
when
\varphi
is not the identity function, under assumption
(A’)
. Finally, we prove Lemma
C.7
on the linearization of
\varphi
around
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}
under
(A’)
which is in particular used in the proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Lemma C.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.2
, if
\log(T)^{3}\|\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\|_{2}=o(1)
, then we have
\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}W_{T}\big((\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\big)=o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(1)
. In particular, for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}

\mathcal{B}_{j,T}=\mathcal{B}_{j}+o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(T^{-1/2})
with
o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(T^{-1/2})
independent of
j
and
\mathcal{B}_{j}=-\langle\tilde{f}^{0}-\tilde{f}^{0,J},\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0,J}_{L,\varphi}\rangle_{L,\varphi}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{j}|\lesssim\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}\|\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}\lesssim\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}\|\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\|_{2}

(C.8)

By assumption, there exists
R>0
such that
\|\psi^{0}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq R/2
and
\sup\{\|\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}.\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{\infty},j\geq 1\}\leq R/2
so that

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\|_{\infty}=\|\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq R.

With this and the first inequality of lemma
E.1
we find that for any
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{T}\cap\big\{\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\big|W_{T}\big((\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\big)\big|>x\big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{T}\cap\big\{\big|W_{T}\big((\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\big)\big|>x\big\}\Big)\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}\exp\bigg(\frac{-\log(T)^{-5}Cx^{2}}{\|(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{Rx}{\sqrt{T}}}\bigg).

Let
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
. Using the equivalence of the norms
\|.\|_{2}
,
\|.\|_{L}
and
\|.\|_{L,\varphi}
and the characterisation of an orthogonal projection, we find:

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\|(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\|_{L}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|P^{j}_{L,\varphi}(\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi})-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|P^{j}_{2}(\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi})-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|P^{j}_{2}(\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi})-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})-\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\|_{2}^{2};

Whence, since
\max\{j,j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}\}\leq J_{T},
we have for some
c,d>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{T}\cap\big\{\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\big|W_{T}\big((\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\big)\big|>x\big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq J_{T}\exp\bigg(\frac{-\log(T)^{-5}Cx^{2}}{c\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\|\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{Rx}{\sqrt{T}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(d\log(T)\bigg(1-\frac{\log(T)^{-6}x^{2}}{c\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\|\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{Rx}{\sqrt{T}}}\bigg)\bigg)\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{}0,

by assumption, which ends the proof of the first assertion.

For the second assertion, let
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
, we have by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{j}|\leq\|\tilde{f}^{0}-\tilde{f}^{0,j}\|_{L,\varphi}\|\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}.

Then, using the same arguments as for the first assertion (namely the equivalence of norms and the characterisation of an orthogonal projection), one can show similarly that
\|\tilde{f}^{0}-\tilde{f}^{0,j}\|_{L,\varphi}\lesssim\|\tilde{f}^{0}-P^{j}_{2}(\tilde{f}^{0})\|_{2}
and
\|\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}-\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}\lesssim\|\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}./\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\|_{2}
. With the
L_{2}
posterior contraction, we must have
\|\tilde{f}^{0}-P^{j}_{2}(\tilde{f}^{0})\|_{2}\lesssim\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}
and it proves the second assertion.
∎

Next, we state and prove Lemma
C.6
on the contraction of the
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}
on
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}
when
\varphi
is not the identity function.

Lemma C.6
.

Under assumptions of theorem
3.2
, we have
\Pi(\mathcal{A}_{T}^{c}|N)=o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(1)
when
\varphi
is not the identity function; where
\mathcal{A}_{T}
is defined in (
3.12
).

By theorem
3.1
and assumption
(A’)
, we obtain that
\Pi(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{T}|N)\rightarrow 1
under
\mathbb{P}_{0}
with

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{T}=\big\{f:\|f-f^{0}\|_{2}\leq\log(T)\varepsilon_{T},\hskip 2.84544pt\forall(l,k)\in[K]^{2}\hskip 2.84544ptRange(h_{l,k})\subset I_{0}(\epsilon)\big\},

where
I_{0}(\epsilon)
is the
\epsilon
-neighbourhood of
I_{0}
, the convex hull of
\cup_{l,k}h_{,lk}^{0}([0,A])
.
Let
f=(\nu,\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))\in\mathcal{A}_{T}^{\prime}
. By again assumption
(A’)
, we know that
\varphi^{-1}
has a bounded derivative on
\varphi^{-1}(I_{0}(\epsilon))
and as a consequence:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\mathbf{h}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{2}\leq\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}.

Whence, choosing the constant
M
large enough in the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{T}
, we have
\mathcal{A}_{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{A}_{T}
and we conclude to the result:

\Pi(\mathcal{A}_{T}|N)\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}1
.
∎Note that by definition of
\mathcal{A}_{T}
and by Lemma
C.3
, we have for
T
large enough

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T}\subset\big\{f:\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq D^{\prime}\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}\big\}

(C.9)

for some
D^{\prime}>0
and
\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}\rightarrow 0
by assumption. Whence, for
T
large enough,
\mathcal{A}_{T}
contains only elements
f
such that for all
l(,k)\in[K]^{2}
,

\displaystyle Range(h_{l,k})\subset I_{0}(\epsilon)\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and}\hskip 8.5359ptRange(\tilde{h}_{l,k})\subset\varphi^{-1}(I_{0}(\epsilon)).

(C.10)

Finally, we turn to the linearization of
\varphi
around the
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}
. Let
f=(\nu,\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{A}_{T}
,
\mathbf{h}=\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})
for some
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}
and for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
write
h_{l,k}-h^{0}_{l,k}=(\tilde{h}_{l,k}-\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k})\bar{\varphi}^{0}_{l,k}+\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{l,k})
. By defining
\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})=(\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{l,k}),(l,k)\in[K]^{2})
, the previous inequalities can be rewritten in vector form as

\displaystyle\mathbf{h}-\mathbf{h}^{0}=(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}+\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})

(C.11)

and we have the following control on the remainder term
\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})
.

Lemma C.7
.

Under the assumptions of theorem
3.2
, for
T
large enough,
let
f=(\nu,\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{A}_{T}
, then equality (
C.11
) holds with
\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})\|_{1}\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}.

Let
f=(\nu,\mathbf{h})=(\nu,\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))\in\mathcal{A}_{T}
, by (
C.10
) we know that for
T
large enough (independently of
f
), for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
and for all
x\in[0,A]
,
h_{l,k}(x)\in I_{0}(\epsilon)
. By assumption
(A’)
, we can do a Taylor-Lagrange expansion, for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
there exists a function
\gamma(\tilde{h}_{l,k},\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k})
in the bracket
[\tilde{h}_{l,k};\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k}]
such that

\displaystyle h_{l,k}-h^{0}_{l,k}=\varphi(\tilde{h}_{l,k})-\varphi(\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k})=\big(\tilde{h}_{l,k}-\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k}\big)\bar{\varphi}^{0}_{l,k}+\frac{\big(\tilde{h}_{l,k}-\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k}\big)^{2}}{2}\varphi^{\prime\prime}\big(\gamma(\tilde{h}_{l,k},\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k})\big)

Let
\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{l,k})=\frac{\big(\tilde{h}_{l,k}-\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k}\big)^{2}}{2}\varphi^{\prime\prime}\big(\gamma(\tilde{h}_{l,k},\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k})\big)
. By assumption,
\varphi^{\prime\prime}
is bounded on
\varphi^{-1}(I_{0}(\epsilon))
so we have
\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{l,k})\|_{1}\lesssim\|\tilde{h}_{l,k}-\tilde{h}_{l,k}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}
and this shows the result.
∎

C.4
Lemmas for the application on specific priors

In this section, we prove three lemmas used to verify conditions of Theorem
3.2
when we apply it to specific prior distributions (random histograms and priors based on wavelet bases) in Section
3.4
. First, the following Lemma
C.8
is used to verify the condition "
\sup\{\|\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{\infty},\hskip 2.84544ptj\geq 1\}<+\infty
" of Theorem
3.2
. Then, Lemma
C.9
shows that the change of variable condition (
3.17
) is verified for the priors considered in Section
3.4
. Finally, we prove in Lemma
C.10
that the assumptions
(P2)
and
(A’)
are satisfied for the priors based on wavelet bases with
\varphi\geq 0
.

Lemma C.8
.

Let
(\xi,\mathbf{g})\in L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
and for
j\geq 1
, let
B_{j}
be a family of
L_{\infty}
linearly independent in
L_{2}
and that verifies (
3.3
). As before, let
(\xi^{j},\mathbf{g}^{j})=P^{j}_{L}(\xi,\mathbf{g})
. Assume that there exists some finite
C(\mathbf{g})
such that for all
j\geq 1

\displaystyle\|P_{2}^{j}(\mathbf{g})\|_{\infty}+j^{\beta}\|P_{2}^{j}(\mathbf{g})-\mathbf{g}\|_{2}\leq C(\mathbf{g}).

Then,
\underset{j\geq 1}{\sup}\|\mathbf{g}^{j}\|_{\infty}<+\infty
.

Let
j\geq 1
. First, with the equivalence between the LAN norm and the
L_{2}
norm and by characterization of an orthogonal projection, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}^{j}-\mathbf{g}\|_{2}\leq\|(\xi^{j},\mathbf{g}^{j})-(\xi,\mathbf{g})\|_{2}\lesssim\|(\xi^{j},\mathbf{g}^{j})-(\xi,\mathbf{g})\|_{L}

\displaystyle\leq\|P_{2}^{j}(\xi,\mathbf{g})-(\xi,\mathbf{g})\|_{L}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|P_{2}^{j}(\xi,\mathbf{g})-(\xi,\mathbf{g})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|P^{2}_{j}(\mathbf{g})-\mathbf{g}\|_{2}.

Then, with this inequality and the conditions of the Lemma we obtain:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}^{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{g}^{j}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g})\|_{\infty}+\|P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{j}\|\mathbf{g}^{j}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g})\|_{2}+C(\mathbf{g})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{j}\|\mathbf{g}^{j}-\mathbf{g}\|_{2}+\sqrt{j}\|\mathbf{g}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g})\|_{2}+C(\mathbf{g})

\displaystyle\lesssim 2\sqrt{j}\|\mathbf{g}-P^{j}_{2}(\mathbf{g})\|_{2}+C(\mathbf{g})

\displaystyle\leq 2j^{1/2-\beta}C(\mathbf{g})+C(\mathbf{g})

Since
\beta>1/2
, we can conclude to the result.
∎

The following lemma shows that the change of variable condition (
3.17
) is verified for the priors considered.

Lemma C.9
.

Consider either the setting of corollary
3.2
(random histograms bases), corollary
3.3
(truncated wavelets bases in ReLU model) or corollary
3.4
(truncated wavelets bases with
\varphi\geq 0
). In all three cases, condition (
3.17
) of Theorem
3.2
is satisfied:

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}\frac{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f_{u,j})}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f)}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}\Pi_{J}(j|N)\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}1.

For the moment we do not fix the prior, it can be either the histogram prior, the wavelet prior in the Relu model or the wavelet prior with
\varphi\geq 0
. First, note that by definition of
\mathcal{A}_{T}
and by Lemma
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T}\subset\big\{f:\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq D\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}\big\},

for some universal
D>0
and
\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}\rightarrow 0
by assumption. So for
T
large enough,
\mathcal{A}_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{R}^{1}
(defined at start of Section
3.1
). Moreover, for
\nu=(\nu_{1},...,\nu_{K})
, let
\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu):=\prod_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{\nu}(\nu_{k})
and, given
j
, for
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{K^{2}j}
recall that
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\prod_{(k,l)\in[K]^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{j}\pi_{\theta,i}(\theta_{l,k}^{i})
. Next, fix
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and denote
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}\in\mathbb{R}^{K^{2}j}
the vector satisfying
\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}^{T}B_{j}
; we set

\displaystyle\Theta_{T}(j):=\big\{(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times\mathbb{R}^{K^{2}j}:(\nu,\varphi(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}B_{j}))\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)\big\},

and
\Theta_{T}^{S}(j)=\Theta_{T}(j)-u(\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j})/\sqrt{T}
. With the change of variable

\displaystyle(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\rightarrow(\nu-u\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}/\sqrt{T},\boldsymbol{\theta}-u\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}/\sqrt{T})

we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f_{u,j})}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f)}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\Theta_{T}(j)}\exp\Big(L_{T}\Big(\nu-u\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}/\sqrt{T},\varphi((\boldsymbol{\theta}-u\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}/\sqrt{T})^{T}B_{j})\Big)\Big)\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu)\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\nu d\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\int_{\Theta_{T}(j)}\exp\Big(L_{T}\big(\nu,\varphi(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}B_{j})\big)\Big)\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu)\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\nu d\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\Theta_{T}^{S}(j)}\exp\Big(L_{T}\big(\nu,\varphi(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}B_{j})\big)\Big)\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu+u\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}/\sqrt{T})\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta}+u\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}/\sqrt{T})d\nu d\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\int_{\Theta_{T}(j)}\exp\Big(L_{T}\big(\nu,\varphi(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}B_{j})\big)\Big)\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu)\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\nu d\boldsymbol{\theta}}.

We first study
\pi_{K,\nu}
.
|\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}|\leq R_{0}
for some
R_{0}>0
independent of
j
and
\|\nu-\nu^{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)
so that for
T
large enough,
\nu^{0}_{k}/2\leq\nu_{k}\leq 3\nu_{k}^{0}/2
for all
k
over
\Theta_{T}^{S}(j)
. Since,
\pi_{\nu}
is positive and continuously differentiable on
]0;+\infty[
,
\log(\pi_{\nu})
is
C^{1}
on
\prod_{k}[\nu_{k}^{0}/2,3\nu_{k}^{0}/2]
and there exists
C_{\pi_{\nu}}>0
such that for
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta_{T}^{S}(j)
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\log\big(\pi_{K,\nu}\big(\nu+u\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}/\sqrt{T}\big)\big)-\log(\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu))\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\big|\log\big(\pi_{\nu}\big(\nu_{k}+u\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi,k}/\sqrt{T}\big)\big)-\log(\pi_{\nu}(\nu_{k}))\big|\lesssim KC_{\pi_{\nu}}R_{0}/\sqrt{T}.

and therefore

\displaystyle\frac{\pi_{K,\nu}\big(\nu+u\xi^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}/\sqrt{T}\big)}{\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu)}=1+o(1)

(C.12)

with
o(1)
independent of
\nu
and
j
. Now, we show similarly that for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta^{S}_{T}(j)
.

\displaystyle\frac{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}+u\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}/\sqrt{T}\big)}{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}=1+o(1),

(C.13)

with again
o(1)
independent of
\boldsymbol{\theta}
and
j
. For this, we treat separately the three priors considered.

For the histogram prior, first note that since
\sup\{\|\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{\infty},j\geq 1\}<+\infty
, there exists some finite
G_{0}>0
such that
\sup\{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}\|_{\infty},j\geq 1\}\leq G_{0}
. Then, recall that the coefficients are distributed independently according to a distribution with a density
\pi_{\theta}
supported on
[\kappa;+\infty[
with
\kappa
such that for some
\delta>0
, we have for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
,
h^{0}_{l,k}>\kappa+\delta
. Moreover, given
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
, by the mean value theorem, there exists
K^{2}

j
-histogram functions whose coefficients
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{*,j}
are between
\kappa+\delta
and
\|\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{\infty}
and such that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{*,j}^{T}B_{j}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)
. Whence, let
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta_{T}(j)
, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{*,j}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{*,j}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{j}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}B_{j}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{*,j}^{T}B_{j}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{J_{T}(\beta)}\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)=o(1).

So, for
T
large enough (independently of
j
),
\Theta_{T}^{S}(j)
contains only elements
\boldsymbol{\theta}
with coefficients that are in some bounded interval
B^{\prime}\subset[\kappa+\delta/2;+\infty[
. Furthermore,
\pi_{\theta}
is positive and continuously differentiable on
]\kappa;+\infty[
. In particular, there exists
C_{\pi_{\theta}}>0
such that for any
x\in B^{\prime}
,
|\pi^{\prime}_{\theta}(x)/\pi_{\theta}(x)|\leq C_{\pi_{\theta}}
. Thus, let
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta_{T}^{S}(j)
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,k)\in[K]^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\Big|\log(\pi_{\theta}(\theta_{l,k}^{i}+u\theta_{0,j,l,k}^{i}/\sqrt{T}))-\log(\pi_{\theta}(\theta_{l,k}^{i}))\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(l,k)\in[K]^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{j}C_{\pi_{\theta}}|u\theta_{0,j,l,k}^{i}|/\sqrt{T}\lesssim C_{\pi_{\theta}}G_{0}\log(T)J_{T}(\beta)/\sqrt{T}=o(1),

and as a consequence, (
C.13
) holds for the histogram prior.

For the wavelet priors, we recall that
\bar{B}_{I}=\big\{\psi_{i,v},i\in\{-1,0,...,I\},v\in\{0,...,\bar{v}_{i}-1\}\big\}
and we recall the notations (
3.24
) that we will use here. As explained in Section
3.4
, a prior on
J
is induced by a prior on
I
through the relation
J=c(I):=\sum_{i=-1}^{I}\bar{v}_{i}
and we let
\bar{\mathbf{g}}^{0,i}_{L,\varphi}=\mathbf{g}^{0,c(i)}_{L,\varphi}=\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}
and we define similarly
\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i}
. The following facts will be used repeatedly in the sequel. If
\mathbf{g}
are
K^{2}
functions belonging to
\mathcal{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta}([0,A])
, their wavelets coefficients at resolution level
i
, denoted
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i}
verify for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
and for all
j\in\{-1,...,i\}
,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i,l,k}^{j}\|_{\infty}\leq M(\mathbf{g})2^{-\max(j,0)(\beta+1/2)}
where
M(\mathbf{g})>0
depends on the
\mathcal{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta}
norm of
\mathbf{g}
. Since
\bar{v}_{j}\lesssim 2^{j}
, it implies that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i,l,k}^{j}\|_{2}\lesssim M(\mathbf{g})2^{-\max(j,0)\beta}.

(C.14)

Moreover, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i}^{T}\bar{B}_{i}-\mathbf{g}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{-i\beta}
. Let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\mathbb{R}^{K^{2}c(i)}
such that
\|\mathbf{g}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}\bar{B}_{i}\|_{2}\leq\gamma
. First, note that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i}\|_{2}=\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}\bar{B}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i}^{T}\bar{B}_{i}\|_{2}\lesssim\gamma+2^{-i\beta}.

Using the previous inequality, we find that for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
and
j\in\{-1,0,...,i\}
;

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l,k}^{j}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l,k}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i,l,k}^{j}\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i,l,k}^{j}\|_{\infty}

(C.15)

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i}\|_{\infty}+M(\mathbf{g})2^{-\max(j,0)(\beta+1/2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma+2^{-i\beta}+M(\mathbf{g})2^{-\max(j,0)(\beta+1/2)},

and, using in addition (
C.14
), we obtain similarly,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l,k}^{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l,k}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i,l,k}^{j}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathbf{g},i,l,k}^{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma+2^{-i\beta}+M(\mathbf{g})2^{-\max(j,0)\beta}

(C.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma+M(\mathbf{g})2^{-\max(j,0)\beta}.

For the wavelet prior in the ReLU model, we proceed similarly to the histogram prior. First, we have
\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i}\|_{2}=\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}^{0,i}_{L,\varphi}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}^{0,i}_{L,\varphi}\|_{\infty}
and since
\sup\{\|\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}^{0,i}_{L,\varphi}\|_{2}\|_{\infty},i\geq-1\}<+\infty
, there exists some finite
G_{0}>0
such that
\sup\{\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i}\|_{\infty},i\geq-1\}\leq G_{0}
. Then, let
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta_{T}(i)
for some
i\in\mathcal{I}_{T}
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}\bar{B}_{i}-\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)
and by (
C.15
) (with
\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{h}^{0}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\boldsymbol{\theta}
) we obtain that for some
C_{0}>0
independent of
i
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}
,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{\infty}\lesssim C_{0}
. Thus, for
T
large enough (independently of
i
),
\Theta_{T}^{S}(i)
contains only elements
\boldsymbol{\theta}
with coefficients that are in some bounded interval
B^{\prime\prime}
, and from that, we can proceed as for the histogram prior and it shows that (
C.13
) is true for the wavelet prior in the ReLU model.

Now, we turn to the wavelet prior with
\varphi\geq 0
, we first treat case (i) (Gaussian priors). Since
\mathbf{g}_{L,\varphi}^{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty,\infty}
,
\|\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L,\varphi}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i}^{T}\bar{B}_{i}\|_{2}=\|\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L,\varphi}-\bar{\mathbf{g}}^{0,i}_{L,\varphi}\|_{2}\lesssim 2^{-i\beta}
. Whence, by applying (
C.16
) (with
\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L,\varphi}
,
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i}
and
\gamma=2^{-i\beta}
), we obtain that for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
, and for all
j\in\{-1,...,i\}
,
\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i,l,k}^{j}\|_{2}\lesssim 2^{-\max(j,0)\beta}
. Then, let
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta_{T}(i)
for some
i\in\mathcal{I}_{T}
(
i\leq I_{T}(\beta)
), by (
C.16
) (with
\mathbf{g}=\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}
,
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\boldsymbol{\theta}
and
\gamma=\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)
) , we have
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{l,k}^{j}\|_{2}\lesssim\log(T)2^{-\max(j,0)\beta}
for all
j\leq i
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|i}\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}+u\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i}/\sqrt{T}\big)}{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|i}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg(-\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{(l,k)\in[K]^{2}}\sum_{j=-1}^{i}2^{\max(j,0)(2\beta_{m}+1)}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{j}}\frac{2u\theta_{l,k}^{j,v}\bar{\theta}_{0,i,l,k}^{j,v}}{2\sqrt{T}}+\frac{(u\bar{\theta}_{0,i,l,k}^{j,v})^{2}}{T}\bigg),

and with the previous remarks, and using that
|2xy|\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
and that
\beta>\beta_{m}
, we find

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=-1}^{i}2^{\max(j,0)(2\beta_{m}+1)}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{j}}\frac{2u\theta_{l,k}^{j,v}\bar{\theta}_{0,i,l,k}^{j,v}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{(u\bar{\theta}_{0,i,l,k}^{j,v})^{2}}{T}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=-1}^{i}2^{\max(j,0)(2\beta_{m}+1)}\Big(\frac{\|\theta_{l,k}^{j}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bar{\theta}_{0,i,l,k}^{j}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{T}}+\frac{\|\bar{\theta}_{0,i,l,k}^{j}\|_{2}^{2}}{T}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(T)^{2}}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{j=-1}^{i}2^{\max(j,0)(2\beta_{m}+1)}2^{-\max(j,0)2\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log(T)^{2}}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{j=-1}^{I_{T}(\beta)}2^{\max(j,0)}\lesssim\frac{\log(T)^{2}2^{I_{T}(\beta)}}{\sqrt{T}}=o(1),

since
2^{I_{T}(\beta)}\asymp T^{1/(2\beta+1)}\log(T)^{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\geq 0
and since again
\beta>\beta_{m}>1/2
. Hence, we can conclude that (
C.13
) is true for the truncated wavelet prior with
\varphi\geq 0
in case (i) (Gaussian distributions).

Finally, let us study case (ii) (uniform distributions). We recall that in this case, for
j\geq 0
,
\bar{\Pi}_{j,v}=\mathcal{U}([-\log(T)^{2}2^{-j/2},\log(T)^{2}2^{-j/2}])
. As in case (i), we have for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
and for all
j\in\{-1,...,i\}
,

\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i,l,k}^{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0,i,l,k}^{j}\|_{2}\lesssim 2^{-\max(j,0)\beta}
. Moreover, let
i\in\mathcal{I}_{T}
and let
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta_{T}(i)
, (
C.15
) gives that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{l,k}^{j}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log(T)2^{-\max(j,0)\beta}
. Whence , since
\beta>\beta_{m}=1/2
there exists
C>0
and
T_{C}
large enough (both independent of
i
), such that for all
T\geq T_{c}
,
\Theta_{T}^{S}(i)
contains only elements
\boldsymbol{\theta}
such that for all
j\in\{0,...,i\}
,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{l,k}^{j}\|_{\infty}\leq C\log(T)2^{-j/2}
. As a consequence, for
T
large enough we have for all
(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta})\in\Theta_{T}^{S}(i)
,
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}+u\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0,j}/\sqrt{T}\big)=\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
,
which proves (
C.13
) in this case.

So (
C.13
) holds for the three priors and with (
C.12
) it implies that, whatever the prior, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f_{u,j})}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f)}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\Theta_{T}^{S}(j)}\exp\Big(L_{T}\big(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}B_{j}\big)\Big)\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu)\pi_{\boldsymbol{\theta}|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\nu d\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\int_{\Theta_{T}(j)}\exp\Big(L_{T}\big(\nu,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}B_{j}\big)\Big)\pi_{K,\nu}(\nu)\pi_{\Theta|j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\nu d\boldsymbol{\theta}}\times\big(1+o(1)\big)

\displaystyle=\Pi\big(\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}(j)\big|N,j,\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)\big)\times\big(1+o(1)\big),

with
o(1)
uniform in
j
and where
\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}(j)=\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)-u\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}/\sqrt{T}
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}=\cup_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}(j)
, as a consequence we find

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}\frac{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f_{u,j})}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}{\int_{\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}e^{L_{T}(f)}d\Pi_{f|j}(f)}\Pi_{J}(j|N)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}\Pi\big(\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}(j)|N,j,\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)\big)\Pi_{J}(j|N)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\Pi\big(\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}|N,\mathcal{A}_{T}\big)=(1+o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(1))\Pi\big(\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}|N\big),

because by lemma
C.6
,
\Pi(\mathcal{A}_{T}|N)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{0}}1
. Since
\sup\{\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{\infty},j\geq 1\}<+\infty
, one can show (as in the proof of theorem 4.2 of
[
14
]
) that by choosing the constant
M
large enough in the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{T}
,
\Pi\big(\mathcal{A}_{T}^{S}|N\big)=1+o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(1)
and it concludes the proof of Lemma
C.9
.
∎

Lemma C.10
.

Consider the setting of corollary
3.4
(truncated wavelet bases with
\varphi\geq 0
), the prior verifies the assumptions
(P2)
and
(A’)
.

Before proving this lemma, we recall a classical Gaussian concentration inequality that can be found in
[
10
]
(theorem 5.6). Let
X_{1},...,X_{n}
be i.i.d. standard and centered Gaussian random variables and
F:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
a
L
-Lipschitz function with respect to the euclidean norm
\|.\|_{2}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then, for any
u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(F(X_{1},...,X_{n})\geq\mathbb{E}[F(X_{1},...,X_{n})]+u\big)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{u^{2}}{2L^{2}}\Big)

(C.17)

We first verify assumption
(P2)
in case (i) (Gaussian distributions) and then we verify it in case (ii) (uniform distributions). In each case, when verifying
(P2)
, we also verify
(A’)
.

In case (i), for
(P2)
, we begin by verifying (
3.9
). Let
r_{T}=r_{T}(\beta)=\sqrt{\log(T)T\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}}
. Given the prior construction and since
\varphi
is Lipschitz and
r_{T}(\beta)\rightarrow+\infty
, it is enough to show that for
I\leq I_{T}(\beta)
and for variables
\boldsymbol{X}=(X_{i,v},i=1,...,I,v=0,...,\bar{v}_{I})\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Big\|\sum_{i=-1}^{I}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{i}}2^{-\max(i,0)(\beta_{m}+1/2)}X_{i,v}\psi_{i,v}\Big\|_{\infty}>r_{T}(\beta)/2\bigg)\leq e^{-w_{T}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}},

(C.18)

for some
w_{T}\rightarrow+\infty
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}^{(i)}=(X_{i,0},....,X_{i,\bar{v}_{i}})
. First, there exists a finite integer
B
, such that for any
x\in[0,A]
and
i\geq-1
,
Card\big(\{v:\psi_{i,v}(x)\neq 0\}\big)\leq B
. Moreover, since
\|\psi_{i,v}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{\max(i,0)/2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i=-1}^{I}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{i}}2^{-\max(i,0)(\beta_{m}+1/2)}X_{i,v}\psi_{i,v}\Big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{-\max(i,0)\beta_{m}}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}.

Furthermore, the application
F:\mathbb{R}^{c(I)}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined by
F(\boldsymbol{x})=\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{-\max(i,0)\beta_{m}}\|\boldsymbol{x}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}
is
c
-Lipschitz with respect to the euclidean norm for some
c>0
and
\mathbb{E}[F(\boldsymbol{X})]\leq C
for some
C>0
. Whence, since
r_{T}(\beta)\rightarrow+\infty
, for
T
large enough and for some
\bar{c}>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Big\|\sum_{i=-1}^{I}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{i}}2^{-\max(i,0)(\beta_{m}+1/2)}X_{i,v}\psi_{i,v}\Big\|_{\infty}>r_{T}(\beta)/2\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{-\max(i,0)\beta_{m}}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\geq\bar{c}r_{T}(\beta)/2\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(F(\boldsymbol{X})\geq\mathbb{E}[F(\boldsymbol{X})]+\bar{c}r_{T}(\beta)/4\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-\bar{c}^{2}r_{T}(\beta)^{2}/(32c^{2})\big)=\exp\big(-\bar{c}^{2}\log(T)T\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}/(32c^{2})\big)\leq\exp\big(-\sqrt{\log(T)}T\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}\big),

and it proves (
C.18
).

We turn to (
3.10
). Let
\bar{c}_{0}=\max_{l,k}\max_{x\in[0,A]}h^{0}_{l,k}(x)
and
\underline{c}_{0}=\min_{l,k}\min_{x\in[0,A]}h^{0}_{l,k}(x)
. For
\epsilon>0
, when it is well-defined, let
U_{\epsilon}:=\varphi^{-1}(]\underline{c}_{0}-\epsilon;\bar{c}_{0}+\epsilon[)
. It is assumed that there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
small enough such that
\varphi
is infinitely differentiable with a positive derivative on
U_{2\epsilon_{0}}=]a_{2\epsilon_{0}};b_{2\epsilon_{0}}[
, for some
b_{2\epsilon_{0}}>a_{2\epsilon_{0}}
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}
be Gaussian variables as before and set
\tilde{h}=\sum_{i=-1}^{I}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{i}}2^{-\max(i,0)(\beta_{m}+1/2)}X_{i,v}\psi_{i,v}
for some
I\leq I_{T}(\beta)
, and
h=\varphi(\tilde{h})
. Similarly to (
C.18
), given the prior construction and since
\varphi
is globally Lipschitz, it is enough to show that for any
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|h-h^{0}_{l,k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\log(T)^{2}r_{T}(\beta)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2},Range(\tilde{h})\not\subset U_{2\epsilon_{0}}\Big)\leq e^{-w_{T}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}},

(C.19)

for some
w_{T}\rightarrow+\infty
. First, consider that for some
x^{\prime}\in[0,A]
,
\tilde{h}_{l,k}(x^{\prime})\leq a_{2\epsilon_{0}}
. Then, let
x\in[0,A]
, for each
i\geq-1
, there is at most
2B
functions
\psi_{i,v}
such that
\psi_{i,v}(x)\neq 0
or
\psi_{i,v}(x^{\prime})\neq 0
, and thus

\displaystyle|\tilde{h}(x)-\tilde{h}(x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=-1}^{I}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{i}}2^{-\max(i,0)(\beta_{m}+1/2)}X_{i,v}\big|\psi_{i,v}(x)-\psi_{i,v}(x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{-\max(i,0)(\beta_{m}+1/2)}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\underset{v=0,...,\bar{v}_{i}}{\max}\big|\psi_{i,v}(x)-\psi_{i,v}(x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim|x-x^{\prime}|\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{-\max(i,0)(\beta_{m}+1/2)}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\underset{v=0,...,\bar{v}_{i}}{\max}\|\psi^{\prime}_{i,v}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim|x-x^{\prime}|\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)(1-\beta_{m})}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim|x-x^{\prime}|\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)/6}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty},

because
\beta_{m}=5/6
. Whence, for some
b>0

\displaystyle\tilde{h}(x)\leq\tilde{h}(x^{\prime})+|\tilde{h}(x)-\tilde{h}(x^{\prime})|\leq a_{2\epsilon_{0}}+|x-x^{\prime}|b\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)/6}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}.

If
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq(a_{\epsilon_{0}}-a_{2\epsilon_{0}})\big(b\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)/6}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\big)^{-1}=:C_{0}(\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}})
, then
\tilde{h}(x)<a_{\epsilon_{0}}
. Moreover, because
\varphi
is globally non decreasing and strictly increasing on
U_{2\varepsilon_{0}}
, if
\tilde{h}(u)<a_{\epsilon_{0}}
, then
\varphi(\tilde{h}_{l,k}^{0}(u))-\varphi(\tilde{h}(u))\geq\epsilon_{0}
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\|h^{0}_{l,k}-h\|_{2}^{2}=\int_{0}^{A}\hskip-5.69046pt\big(\varphi(\tilde{h}^{0}_{l,k}(u))-\varphi(\tilde{h}(u))\big)^{2}du\geq\epsilon_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{A}\hskip-5.69046pt\mathds{1}_{\big\{|u-x^{\prime}|\leq C_{0}(\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}})\}}du\geq\epsilon_{0}^{2}C_{0}(\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}).

This reasoning can be reproduced in the case where for some
x^{\prime}
,
\tilde{h}(x^{\prime})\geq b_{2\epsilon_{0}}
. It proves that for some
\bar{b}_{0}>0
and
T
large enough

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|h-h^{0}_{l,k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\log(T)^{2}r_{T}(\beta)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2},Range(\tilde{h})\not\subset U_{2\epsilon_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)/6}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{\bar{b}_{0}}{\log(T)^{2}r_{T}(\beta)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}}\Big).

Now, let
\bar{F}(\boldsymbol{X})=\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)/6}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}
.
\bar{F}
is
4\times 2^{I_{T}(\beta)/6}
-Lipschitz for
T
large enough and
2^{I_{T}(\beta)/6}\asymp T^{\frac{1/6}{2\beta+1}}\log(T)^{\frac{\beta/2+1/6}{2\beta+1}}
. Moreover,
\mathbb{E}[\bar{F}(\boldsymbol{X})]\lesssim\log(T)^{2}T^{\frac{1/6}{2\beta+1}}
and
\log(T)^{4}T^{\frac{1/6}{2\beta+1}}r_{T}(\beta)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}\rightarrow 0
because
\beta>5/6
. So, for
T
large enough, with the Gaussian concentration inequality (
C.17
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)/2}\|\boldsymbol{X^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{\bar{b}_{0}}{\log(T)^{2}r_{T}(\beta)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\bar{F}(\boldsymbol{X})\geq\mathbb{E}[\bar{F}(\boldsymbol{X})]+\frac{\bar{b}_{0}}{2\log(T)^{2}r_{T}(\beta)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\frac{-\bar{b}_{0}^{2}}{8\log(T)^{4}T^{\frac{1/3}{2\beta+1}}r_{T}(\beta)^{4}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{4}}\bigg)\leq\exp\big(-\log(T)T\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}\big),

where for the last inequality we have used again that
\beta>\beta_{m}=5/6
. It concludes the proof for (
3.10
).

Since we have proven that (
C.19
) holds, assumption
(A’)
is verified too and it the proof terminates for case
(i)
.

Now, we treat case (ii) (uniform distributions). In this case
\beta_{m}=1/2
. We begin again by verifying (
3.9
). Similarly to case
(i)
, given the prior construction and since
\varphi
is Lipschitz, it is enough to show that for
I\leq I_{T}(\beta)
and for variables
\boldsymbol{U}=(U_{i,v},i=1,...,I,v=0,...,\bar{v}_{I})\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}\mathcal{U}([-1,1])
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{-1}}C_{0}U_{-1,v}\psi_{-1,v}+\sum_{i=0}^{I}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{i}}\log(T)^{2}2^{-i/2}U_{i,v}\psi_{i,v}\Big\|_{\infty}\leq r_{T},

(C.20)

for some
r_{T}>0
. Let
\boldsymbol{U}^{(i)}=(U_{i,0},....,U_{i,\bar{v}_{i}})
. With similar arguments as for case (i), we find that

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{-1}}C_{0}U_{-1,v}\psi_{-1,v}+\sum_{i=0}^{I}\sum_{v=0}^{\bar{v}_{i}}\log(T)^{2}2^{-i/2}U_{i,v}\psi_{i,v}\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\sum_{i=-1}^{I}\|\boldsymbol{U}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log(T)^{3}.

Whence, choosing
r_{T}=C\log(T)^{3}
for some
C>0
large enough, (
C.20
) is proved.

Then, to verify (
3.10
), following the same steps as in case (i), it is sufficient that for some
w_{T}\rightarrow+\infty

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|h-h^{0}_{l,k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C^{2}\log(T)^{6}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2},Range(\tilde{h})\not\subset U_{2\epsilon_{0}}\Big)\leq e^{-w_{T}T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}},

(C.21)

and this holds as soon as we have for some
\bar{b}_{0}^{\prime}>0
chosen large enough

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)}\|\boldsymbol{U^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{\bar{b}_{0}^{\prime}}{\log(T)^{8}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}}\Big)\leq\exp(-w_{T}T\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}).

(C.22)

But, note that
\sum_{i=-1}^{I}2^{\max(i,0)}\|\boldsymbol{U^{(i)}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{I_{T}(\beta)}\lesssim T^{1/(2\beta+1)}\log(T)^{\alpha}
for some
\alpha>0
. Moreover, since
\beta>1/2
, for any
\Delta>0
we have for
T
enough

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log(T)^{8}\varepsilon_{T}(\beta)^{2}}>\Delta T^{1/(2\beta+1)}\log(T)^{\alpha},

so that the probability on the left-hand side of (
C.22
) is equal to
0
for
T
large enough. Therefore (
3.10
) is verified.

Finally, as in case (i), assumption
(A’)
is verified since (
C.21
) holds and it terminates the proof of the lemma.
∎

D
Proofs and additional results for Section
4

D.1
Proof of Lemma
4.1

First, recall that
\Gamma
is linear and bijective operator of
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{2}^{K^{2}}
. As
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{2}^{K^{2}}
,
\Gamma
is also a linear bijective operator of
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
to
\Gamma(\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}})
. Moreover, it is shown in the proof of Lemma
D.2
that for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
and
g\in L_{2}
we have
\|\zeta_{l,j,k}(g)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|g\|_{1}
. So if
g\in L_{\infty}
, we have
\|\zeta_{l,j,k}(g)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|g\|_{\infty}
. With this last inequality, the upper and lower bounds on
p_{l,k}
stated in Lemma
D.2
and using the expression of Lemma
2.4
, one can show that there exists
c>0
such that have that for all
(\xi,\mathbf{g})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
,
\|\Gamma(\xi,\mathbf{g})\|_{\infty}\leq c\|(\xi,\mathbf{g})\|_{\infty}
. In other words,
\Gamma
is a bounded operator from
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
to
\Gamma(\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}})\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
. It remains to show that
\Gamma(\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}})=\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
. Let
(\xi,\mathbf{g})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
, using again Lemma
D.2
and the converse expression (
2.13
), one can again show similarly that
\Gamma^{-1}(\xi,\mathbf{g})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
. It shows that
\Gamma^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}})\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
so
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}\subseteq\Gamma(\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}})
and we can conclude to the equality of these two spaces.

Now, we turn to the operator
\Gamma^{e}
on
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L^{K^{2}}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. First, we have the follwing fact that we call
(*)
for the remaining of the proof:
(\zeta_{l,j,k}^{e}(g_{j,k}),(l,j,k)\in[K]^{3})
and
(\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,\mathbf{g}_{k}),k\in[K])
depends only on the values of the functions
\mathbf{g}
for
x\in[0,A]
. With
(*)
it comes that for
\mathbf{g}\in L^{K^{2}}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
\Gamma^{e}(\xi,\mathbf{g})_{|[0,A]}=\Gamma(\xi,\mathbf{g}_{|[0,A]})
. Moreover, as Lemma
D.2
is written for
p^{e}_{l,k}
and
\zeta^{e}_{l,j,k}
, the same argument used for
\Gamma
shows that
\Gamma^{e}
is a bounded linear operator on
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L^{K^{2}}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. Next, we prove that this operator is injective. Let
(\xi^{a},\mathbf{g}^{a}),(\xi^{b},\mathbf{g}^{b})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L^{K^{2}}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\Gamma^{e}(\xi^{b},\mathbf{g}^{b})=\Gamma^{e}(\xi^{a},\mathbf{g}^{a})
, in particular

\displaystyle\Gamma(\xi^{b},\mathbf{g}^{b}_{|[0,A]})=\Gamma^{e}(\xi^{b},\mathbf{g}^{b})_{|[0,A]}=\Gamma^{e}(\xi^{b},\mathbf{g}^{b})_{|[0,A]}=\Gamma(\xi^{a},\mathbf{g}^{a}_{|[0,A]})

and consequently,
(\xi^{b},\mathbf{g}^{b}_{|[0,A]})=(\xi^{a},\mathbf{g}^{a}_{|[0,A]})
as
\Gamma
is injective. By
(*)
, for
i\in\{a,b\}
, the restricted solution
(\xi^{i},\mathbf{g}^{i}_{[0,A]})
defines for all
(l,j,k)\in[K]^{3}
the function
\zeta_{l,j,k}^{e}(g^{i}_{j,k})
on the whole real line, and thus
\zeta_{l,j,k}^{e}(g^{a}_{j,k})=\zeta_{l,j,k}^{e}(g^{b}_{j,k})
. Then, as the functions
p^{e}_{l,k}
are bounded away from
0
and
+\infty
by Lemma
D.2
, we deduce by definition of
\Gamma^{e}
that
\mathbf{g}^{a}=\mathbf{g}^{b}
on the whole real line, which ends to show that
\Gamma^{e}
is injective. It remains to show that it is also surjective. Let
(\xi^{\prime},\mathbf{g}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}(\mathbb{R})
, since
\Gamma
is surjective on
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
, there exists
(\xi,\mathbf{g}_{A})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
such that
(\xi^{\prime},\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{[0,A]})=\Gamma(\xi,\mathbf{g}_{A})
. By
(*)
again,
\mathbf{g}_{A}
defines the function
\zeta^{e}_{l,j,k}(g_{A,l,k})
on the whole real line. Let
(\xi,\mathbf{g})
be defined by
(\xi,\mathbf{g}_{|[0,A]})=(\xi,\mathbf{g}_{A})
and for
x\in\mathbb{R}\backslash[0,A]
:

\displaystyle g_{l,k}(x)=\frac{g^{\prime}_{l,k}(x)-\mu^{0}_{l}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\zeta^{e}(g_{A,j,k})(x)}{\mu^{0}_{l}p^{e}_{l,k}(x)}-\xi_{k}\hskip 2.84544pt,\hskip 5.69046pt\forall(l,k)\in K^{2}

Then,
(\xi,\mathbf{g})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}(\mathbb{R})
and we have
\Gamma^{e}(\xi,\mathbf{g})=(\xi^{\prime},\mathbf{g}^{\prime})
. Thus,
\Gamma^{e}
is surjective and by Banach-Schauder theorem,
(\Gamma^{e})^{-1}
is bounded.

D.2
On the first order Palm distribution

Lemma D.1
.

Let
N
be a stationary multivariate Hawkes process with parameters
f^{0}\in]0,+\infty[^{K}\times\mathcal{H}
, such that
\mathbf{h}^{0}\in L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
. The Palm first moment measure of the process is given by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(0,l)}_{0}[N^{k}(B)]=\mathds{1}_{0\in B,l=k}+\int_{B}m_{l,k}(u)du,

with
m_{l,k}
bounded. Moreover, if the functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
for some
\beta>0
, then the functions
(m_{l,k},(l,k)\in[K]^{2})
are in
C^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})
.

For
x\in\mathbb{R}
, by (
A.3
), we thus have
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}_{0}[N^{k}(B)]=\mathds{1}_{x\in B,l=k}+\int_{B}m_{l,k}(u-x)du
.

We know by proposition 13.2.VI of
[
20
]
that
\mathbb{E}^{(0,l)}_{0}[N^{k}(B)]=\bar{M}_{l,k}^{2}(B)/\mu^{0}_{l}
,
with
\bar{M}^{2}
is the reduced second moment measure of the Hawkes process. Then, using equation (7) of
[
33
]
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\bar{M}_{l,k}^{2}(B)=\mu^{0}_{l}\mathds{1}_{0\in B,k=l}+\mu^{0}_{l}\mu^{0}_{k}|B|+\int_{B}\Upsilon_{l,k}(t)dt,

with
\Upsilon
verifying
\Upsilon(-t)=\Upsilon(t)^{T}
and satisfying the following functional equation:

\displaystyle\Upsilon(t)=\mathbf{h}^{0,T}(t)D(\mu^{0})+(\mathbf{h}^{0,T}\star\Upsilon)(t)\hskip 8.5359pt,\hskip 14.22636ptt>0,

where
D(\mu^{0})
is the diagonal matrix with
\mu^{0}
as diagonal vector and
\star
is the matrix product where all the multiplications are replaced by convolutions. Using that the functions
h^{0}_{l,k}
are bounded, direct computations show that the functions
\Upsilon_{l,k}
are also bounded. Whence,

\mathbb{E}^{(0,l)}_{0}[N^{k}(.)]
is the sum of the Dirac
\mathds{1}\{0\in.,k=l\}
and a measure absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure with a bounded Radon–Nikodym derivative given by
m_{l,k}(t):=\mu_{k}^{0}+\Upsilon_{l,k}(t)/\mu_{l}^{0}
. Finally, given the functional equation verified by
\Upsilon
, it is clear that when the functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
, the functions
(m_{l,k},(l,k)\in[K]^{2})
are in
C^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})
.
∎

D.3
Boundedness and smoothness of
p^{e}_{l,k}
and
\zeta^{e}_{l,j,k}

In this section, we prove two lemmas on the functions
p^{e}_{l,k}
and the operators
\zeta^{e}_{l,j,k}
defined by (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) respectively. Both depend on the extended functions
\mathbf{h}^{0,e}
and we recall that these functions are bounded on
\mathbb{R}
and for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
,
h^{0,e}_{l,k}\geq-\nu^{0}_{k}/2
.

Lemma D.2
.

For all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
, the function
p_{l,k}^{e}
is upper bounded and lower bounded by some positive constant
c_{l,k}
. Moreover, there exists
C>0
such that for any
g:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
g_{|[0,A]}
is in
L_{2}([0,A])
, for all
(l,k,j)\in[K]^{3}
,
\|\zeta_{l,j,k}^{e}(g)\|_{\infty}\leq C\|g_{|[0,A]}\|_{1}
.

Let
(l,k,j)\in[K]^{3}
. We begin with the function
p_{l,k}^{e}
. Clearly,
p_{l,k}^{e}\leq 2/\nu^{0}_{k}
which gives the upper bounded. For the lower bound, we have by Jensen inequality, formula (
A.3
) and Lemma
D.1
:

\displaystyle p_{l,k}^{e}(A-u)=\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(u,l)}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(u,l))}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(u,l)}\bigg[\frac{1}{2\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{h}^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty}N([0,A])}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\Big(2\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{h}^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}^{(u,l)}_{0}[N([0,A])]\Big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Big(2\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{h}^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}^{(0,l)}_{0}[N([-u,A-u])]\Big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\geq\Big(2\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbf{h}^{0}_{k}\|_{\infty}\big(1+A\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|m_{l,k}\|_{\infty}\Big)^{-1}=:c_{l,k}.

So, we have shown that
p_{l,k}^{e}\geq c_{l,k}>0
.

For the functions
\zeta_{l,j,k}^{e}(g)
, with again Lemma
D.1
we have

\displaystyle|\zeta_{l,j,k}^{e}(g)(A-u)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\nu^{0}_{k}}\mathbb{E}^{(u,l)}_{0}\bigg[\int_{0}^{A}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(u,l)}|g(A-s)|dN_{s}^{j}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\nu^{0}_{k}}\mathbb{E}^{(0,l)}_{0}\bigg[\int_{-u}^{A-u}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(0,l)}|g(A-u-s)|dN_{s}^{j}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\nu^{0}_{k}}\int_{-u}^{A-u}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(0,l)}|g(A-u-s)|m_{l,j}(s)ds\leq\frac{2\|m_{l,j}\|_{\infty}}{\nu^{0}_{k}}\|g_{|[0,A]}\|_{1},

and it proves the result.
∎

The next lemma is on the smoothness of the functions
p_{l,k}^{e}
when it is assumed that the functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
. We recall that in this case, the extended functions
\mathbf{h}^{0,e}
are in
C^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma D.3
.

If the functions
h^{0}_{l,k}
belong to
C^{\beta}([0,A])
for some
\beta\in]0,1]
, then the functions
p_{l,k}^{e}
belong to
C_{b}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})
.

By Lemma
D.2
, we already know that the functions
p_{l,k}^{e}
are in
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, we just have to show that they are also in
C^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})
. Let
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. Without loss of generality, assume that
x<y
. First, if
y-x>A
, because
p_{l,k}^{e}
is bounded, we have directly
|p_{l,k}^{e}(y)-p_{l,k}^{e}(x)|\lesssim|y-x|
. Now, we consider that
y-x\leq A
. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l)):=\nu^{0}_{k}+\sum_{j=1}^{K}\int_{t-A}^{t}\mathds{1}_{(u,j)\neq(x,l)}h^{0}_{j,k}(t-u)dN_{u}^{j}.

(D.1)

Since
N
has a fixed atom at
(x,l)
under
\mathbb{P}^{(x,l)}
(see Appendix
A
), we have under
\mathbb{P}^{(x,l)}

\displaystyle\lambda_{t}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(x,l))=\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l))+h^{0}_{l,k}(t-x)\mathds{1}_{x\in[t-A,t)}+h^{0,e}_{l,k}(t-x)\mathds{1}_{x\notin[t-A,t)},

and thus

\displaystyle p_{l,k}^{e}(A-x)=\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l))+h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-x)}\bigg].

Then, using in addition formula (
A.3
), we also have:

\displaystyle p_{l,k}^{e}(A-y)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{(y,l)}_{0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})+h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-y)\mathds{1}_{y\notin[0,A[}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}_{0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A+x-y}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})+h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-y)\mathds{1}_{y\notin[0,A[}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}_{0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A+x-y}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l))+h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-y)}\bigg].

For
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle\Delta_{\delta}(k,x,l)

\displaystyle:=\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(x,l))-\lambda_{A-\delta}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(x,l)),

(D.2)

and note that under
\mathbb{P}^{(x,l)}
, when
\delta>0

\displaystyle\Delta_{\delta}(k,x,l)=h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-x)-h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-x-\delta)+\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l))-\lambda_{A-\delta}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l))

(D.3)

\displaystyle=\big(h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-x)-h^{0,e}_{l,k}(A-x-\delta)\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{A-\delta}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}(h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)-h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s-\delta))dN^{j}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{K}-\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s-\delta)dN^{j}_{s}+\sum_{j=1}^{K}\int_{A-\delta}^{A}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)dN^{j}_{s}

and if
\delta<0
, the same decomposition holds after adapting the limits of integration. Since the functions
h^{0,e}_{l,k}
are
\beta
-Hölder on
\mathbb{R}
, we find

\displaystyle|p_{l,k}^{e}(A-y)-p_{l,k}^{e}(A-x)|\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\Big[|\Delta_{y-x}(k,x,l)|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\lesssim|y-x|^{\beta}+\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\Big[\big|\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l))-\lambda_{A+x-y}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(x,l))\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\lesssim|y-x|^{\beta}+|y-x|^{\beta}\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\sum_{j\neq l}N^{j}([0,A+x-y])+N^{l}([0,A+x-y]\backslash\{x\})\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 2.84544pt+\|\mathbf{h}^{0}\|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\sum_{j\neq l}N^{j}([x-y,0]\cup[A+x-y,A])+N^{l}\big(([x-y,0]\cup[A+x-y,A])\backslash\{x\}\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\lesssim|y-x|^{\beta}+|y-x|,

where for the last inequality we have used Lemma
D.1
on the Palm first moment measure. It ends to prove that the function
p^{e}_{l,k}
is in
C^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})
.
∎

D.4
Extension to the case
\beta>1

In this section we aim to prove that when functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
and
\mathbf{g}^{0}_{2}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
for some
\beta>1
, then the functions
\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}
are also in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
(it extends corollary
4.1
to the case
\beta>1
). We recall that
\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}
verifies
