\displaystyle g^{0}_{L,l,k}=\frac{g^{0}_{2,l,k}-\mu^{0}_{l}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\zeta_{l,j,k}(g^{0}_{L,j,k})}{\mu^{0}_{l}p_{l,k}}-\xi^{0}_{L,k}\hskip 2.84544pt,\hskip 5.69046pt\forall(l,k)\in[K]^{2}.

We want to show, using the second order Palm distribution, that the functions
p_{l,k}
and
\zeta_{l,j,k}(g^{0}_{L,j,k})
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
and since the functions
p_{l,k}
are bounded away from
0
(Lemma
D.2
), it would prove the desired result. In particular, by applying (
A.2
), we obtain for the most problematic term
\zeta_{l,j,k}(g^{0}_{L,j,k})
a more explicit expression involving the second order Palm distribution:

\displaystyle\zeta_{l,j,k}(g^{0}_{L,j,k})(A-x)=\int_{0}^{A}g^{0}_{L,j,k}(A-s)\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,s,l,j)}\Big[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big]m_{l,j}(s-x)ds,

(D.4)

where we recall that
\mathbb{E}^{(x,u,l,j)}_{0}
is an expectation under the second order Palm distribution (see the Appendix
A
). However, our approach requires certain regularity properties on the second order Palm distribution that we have not been able to establish but that we believe to hold. We conjecture these regularity properties.

Before stating the conjecture, we recall and introduce some notations. We denote by
\|.\|_{C^{\beta}}
the usual Hölder norm on
[0,A]
. A map
x\mapsto\phi(x,s)
will be sometimes written for short
\phi(\cdot{,}s)
. Furthermore, we denote by
\partial h
and
\partial^{m}h
the first derivative and the
m
-th derivative of a function
h
, respectively. Recall the notation defined
\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(s,j))
by (
D.1
). Let
x,s
in a neighborhood of
[0,A]
,
(l,j,k)\in[K]^{3}
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
, and for
r\geq 1

0\leq i\leq r-1
and
c\geq 0
, we define

\displaystyle p^{[2]}_{r,k}(x,s,l,j):=\mathbb{E}^{(x,s,l,j)}_{0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{r}}\bigg],

\displaystyle p^{![2]}_{r,i,c,k}(x,s,l,j):=\mathbb{E}^{(x,s,l,j)}_{0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(s,j))^{r-i}\big(\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(s,j))+c\big)^{i+1}}\bigg].

In the following conjecture, the regularity properties must hold for any
(l,j,k)\in[K]^{3}
,
r\in\{1,...,\lfloor\beta\rfloor\}
,
i\in\{0,...,r-1\}
and
c\geq 0
.

Conjecture 1
.

Consider that the functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
for some
\beta>1
. We conjecture that for all
x\in[0,A]
, there exists a version of the function
s\mapsto p^{![2]}_{r,i,c,k}(x,s,l,j)
which is continuous at
A
and at
0
. Moreover, we conjecture that there are versions of the functions
x\mapsto p^{![2]}_{r,i,c,k}(x,A,l,j)
and
x\mapsto p^{![2]}_{r,i,c,k}(x,0,l,j)
that are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
, and that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{A}\|p^{[2]}_{r,k}(\cdot{,}s,l,j)\|_{C^{\beta}}\hskip 2.84544ptds<+\infty.

Since the second order Palm distribution is symmetric in
(x,s)
, we think that if one can show that the functions
x\mapsto\mathbb{E}^{(x,s,l,j)}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{-1}]
are
\beta
-Hölder, then one can show similarly that this conjecture holds. Note that this conjecture holds in the Poisson case (namely when
\mathbf{h}^{0}=0
) since the second order Palm distribution of a Poisson process at
(x,s,l,j)
is just the convolution between the original Poisson process, a Dirac measure at
(x,l)
and a Dirac measure at
(s,j)
(see Lemma 6.15 of
[
37
]
for instance).

Under Conjecture
1
, with (
D.4
) and since by Lemma
D.1
there exists
L>0
such that for all
s\in[0,A]
the function
x\rightarrow m_{l,j}(s-x)
is
(L,\beta)
-Hölder, it is clear that the functions
\zeta_{l,j,k}(g^{0}_{L,j,k})
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
. Then, we show that the functions
p_{l,k}
are also in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
under this conjecture.

Lemma D.4
.

If the functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
for some
\beta>1
, then, under Conjecture
1
, the functions
(p_{l,k},(l,k)\in[K]^{2})
are also in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
.

To prove this lemma, we introduce some notations to shorten the computations. For a random variable
X_{\delta}
that depends on some
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
, we will denote
X_{\delta}=O_{\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}}(\delta)
and
X_{\delta}=o_{\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}}(\delta)
when, at
\delta=0
,
\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}[|X_{\delta}|]=O(\delta)
and
\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}[|X_{\delta}|]=o(\delta)
respectively. For
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
and
r\in\{1,...,\lfloor\beta\rfloor\}
, we set

\displaystyle p^{[1]}_{r,k}(A-x,l)=\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}_{0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{r}}\bigg].

(D.5)

Note that
p^{[1]}_{1,k}(x,l)=p_{l,k}(x)
. Finally, we set

\displaystyle p^{![2],0}_{k}(x,s,l,j):=p^{![2]}_{1,0,h^{0}_{j,k}(0),k}(x,s,l,j)\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and}\hskip 8.5359ptp^{![2],A}_{k}(x,s,l,j):=p^{![2]}_{1,0,h^{0}_{j,k}(A),k}(x,s,l,j).

We first differentiate the functions
p_{l,k}
. Let
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
,
x\in[0,A]
and
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
with
\delta>0
if
x=0
and
\delta<0
if
x=A
and
\delta
is small enough so that
x+\delta\in[0,A]
. With similar computations as in the proof of Lemma
D.3
and since the functions
h^{0}_{l,k}
are
\beta
-Hölder, we find that for
\delta>0

\displaystyle p_{l,k}(A-x-\delta)-p_{l,k}(A-x)=\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}_{0}\bigg[\frac{\Delta^{+}_{\delta}(k,x,l)}{\lambda_{A-\delta}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg],

with

\displaystyle\Delta^{+}_{\delta}(k,x,l)=\underbrace{\delta\partial h^{0}_{l,k}(A-x)+\delta\sum_{j=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{A-\delta}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}\partial h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)dN^{j}_{s}}_{\Delta_{\delta}(\partial\mathbf{h}^{0},l,k)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{K}h^{0}_{j,k}(A)\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}+h^{0}_{j,k}(0)\int_{A-\delta}^{A}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}+O_{\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}}(\delta^{\beta_{d}}),

where
\beta_{d}=\min(2,\beta)>1
. If
\delta<0
, the same decomposition holds after replacing
\Delta^{+}_{\delta}(k,x,l)
by
\Delta^{-}_{\delta}(k,x,l)
given by

\displaystyle\Delta^{-}_{\delta}(k,x,l)=\delta\partial h^{0}_{l,k}(A-x)+\delta\sum_{j=1}^{K}\int_{-\delta}^{A^{-}}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}\partial h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)dN^{j}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{K}h^{0}_{j,k}(A)\int_{0}^{-\delta}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}-h^{0}_{j,k}(0)\int_{A}^{A-\delta}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}+O_{\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}}(\delta^{\beta_{d}}).

Using again that the functions
h^{0}_{l,k}
are
\beta
-Hölder and (
A.4
), we find when
\delta>0

\displaystyle p_{l,k}(A-x-\delta)-p_{l,k}(A-x)=\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\frac{\Delta_{\delta}(\partial\mathbf{h}^{0},l,k)}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}\bigg]

(D.6)

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{K}h^{0}_{j,k}(A)\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\frac{\int_{-\delta}^{0^{-}}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})(\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})+h^{0}_{j,k}(A)\int_{-\delta}^{0^{-}}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s})}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{K}h^{0}_{j,k}(0)\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\frac{\int_{A-\delta}^{A^{-}}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})(\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})-h^{0}_{j,k}(0)\int_{A-\delta}^{A^{-}}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s})}\bigg]+O(\delta^{\beta_{d}})

Moreover, by Lemma
D.5
, for all
j\in[K]

\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}=\left(\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}\right)\mathds{1}_{\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}=1}+o_{\mathbb{E}^{(x,l)}}(\delta),

so that for all
j

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k}))(\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})+h^{0}_{j,k}(A)\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\frac{\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}dN^{j}_{s}}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})(\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})+h^{0}_{j,k}(A))}\bigg]+o(\delta).

Doing similarly for the third term on the right-hand side of (
D.6
) and then applying (
A.2
), it leads to

\displaystyle p_{l,k}(A-x-\delta)-p_{l,k}(A-x)

\displaystyle=\delta\partial h^{0}_{l,k}(A-x)p^{[1]}_{2,k}(x,l)+\delta\sum_{j}\int_{0}^{A-}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}\partial h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)p^{[2]}_{2,k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\underbrace{\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(A)\int_{-\delta}^{0}\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,s,l,j)}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})(\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})+h_{j,k}^{0}(A))}\bigg]m_{l,j}(s-x)ds}_{F_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\underbrace{\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(0)\int_{A-\delta}^{A}\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,s,l,j)}\bigg[\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})(\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})-h_{j,k}^{0}(0))}\bigg]m_{l,j}(s-x)ds}_{F_{2}}+o(\delta).

Now, recall from the Appendix
A
that
\mathbb{P}^{(x,s,l,j)}\big(N^{j}(\{s\})=1\big)=1
, so when
s\in[A-\delta,A]
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(s,j))+h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)\hskip 2.84544pt,\hskip 5.69046pt\mathbb{P}^{(x,s,l,j)}-a.s.

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k},!(s,j))+h^{0}_{j,k}(0)+O(\delta)\hskip 2.84544pt,\hskip 5.69046pt\mathbb{P}^{(x,s,l,j)}-a.s.,

with
\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},!(s,j))
defined by (
D.1
). Furthermore, when
s\in[-\delta,0[
,
\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k})=\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},!(s,j))
. Whence,

\displaystyle F_{1}=-\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(A)\int_{-\delta}^{0}p^{![2],A}_{k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds,

\displaystyle F_{2}=\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(0)\int_{A-\delta}^{A}p^{![2],0}_{k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds+O(\delta^{2}).

When
\delta<0
, doing the same, we find that

\displaystyle p_{l,k}(A-x-\delta)-p_{l,k}(A-x)

\displaystyle=\delta\partial h^{0}_{l,k}(A-x)p^{[1]}_{2,k}(A-x,l)+\delta\sum_{j}\int_{0}^{A-}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}\partial h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)p^{[2]}_{2,k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(A)\int_{0}^{-\delta}p^{![2],A}_{k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(0)\int^{A-\delta}_{A}p^{![2],0}_{k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds+O(\delta^{2}).

By Lemma
D.1
, the functions
s\rightarrow m_{l,j}(s-x)
are
\beta
-Hölder. Thus, with in addition Conjecture
1
, we have that

\displaystyle\partial p_{l,k}(A-x)=-\underset{\delta\rightarrow 0}{\lim}\frac{p_{l,k}(A-x-\delta)-p_{l,k}(A-x)}{\delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt=-\partial h^{0}_{l,k}(A-x)p^{[1]}_{2,k}(x,l)-\sum_{j}\int_{0}^{A-}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}\partial h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)p^{[2]}_{2,k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt+\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(A)p^{![2],A}_{k}(x,0,l,j)m_{l,j}(-x)-\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(0)p^{![2],0}_{k}(x,A,l,j)m_{l,j}(A-x).

Now, we study the regularity of this derivative. Conjecture
1
implies that if
x\mapsto p^{[1]}_{2,k}(x,l)
is
(\beta-1)
-Hölder, then
\partial p^{e}_{l,k}
is also
(\beta-1)
-Hölder, which proves the result. So it remains to study the regularity of
x\mapsto p^{[1]}_{2,k}(x,l)
. Using
a^{r}-b^{r}=(a-b)\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}a^{r-1-i}b^{i}
, we have for
r\geq 2
and
\delta>0

\displaystyle p^{[1]}_{r,k}(A-x-\delta,l)-p^{[1]}_{r,k}(A-x,l)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}E^{(x,l)}\bigg[\frac{\Delta^{+}_{\delta}(k,x,l)}{\lambda_{A-\delta}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k})^{1+i}\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k})^{r-i}}\bigg].

Thus, using what we just did for
p_{l,k}(x)=p^{[1]}_{1,k}(x,l)
, one can show similarly that for
r\in\{2,....\lfloor\beta\rfloor\}
, the function
x\rightarrow p^{[1]}_{r,k}(x,l)
is differentiable with

\displaystyle\partial p^{[1]}_{r,k}(A-x,l)=-r\partial h^{0}_{l,k}(A-x)p^{[1]}_{r+1,k}(x,l)

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt-r\sum_{j}\int_{0}^{A-}\mathds{1}_{(s,j)\neq(x,l)}\partial h^{0}_{j,k}(A-s)p^{[2]}_{r+1,k}(x,s,l,j)m_{l,j}(s-x)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt+\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(A)p^{![2]}_{r,i,h^{0}_{j,k}(A),k}(x,0,l,j)m_{l,j}(-x)

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt-\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}\sum_{j}h_{j,k}^{0}(0)p^{![2]}_{r,i,h^{0}_{j,k}(0),k}(x,A,l,j)m_{l,j}(A-x),

so that if
p^{[1]}_{r+1,k}(\cdot{,}l)
is Lipschitz then
\partial p^{[1]}_{r,k}(\cdot{,}l)
is Lipschitz. Moreover, by Lemma
D.6
, for all
r\geq 2
,
p^{[1]}_{r,k}(\cdot{,}l)
is Lipschitz. In particular,
p^{[1]}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1,k}(\cdot{,}l)
is Lipschitz and thus
\partial^{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor-1)}p^{[1]}_{2,k}(\cdot{,}l)
is Lipschitz. This proves that
p^{[1]}_{2,k}(\cdot{,}l)
is
\beta
-Hölder, which concludes the proof.
∎

We state and prove two results used in the previous proof of Lemma
D.4
. The first one is on the orderliness of the process under the first order Palm distribution.

Lemma D.5
.

Let
(x,l)\in\mathbb{R}\times[K]
,
z\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
B_{z,\delta}:=\big\{s\in\mathbb{R}:|s-z|\leq|\delta|\big\}
and
B_{z,\delta}^{-x}=B_{z,\delta}\backslash\{x\}
. Then,
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{(x,l)}\big(N^{l}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})\geq 2\big)\lesssim\delta^{2}
and for
j\neq l
,
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{(x,l)}\big(N^{j}(B_{z,\delta})\geq 2\big)\lesssim\delta^{2}

First, note that when
j\neq l
,
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{(x,l)}\big(N^{j}(B_{z,\delta})=N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})\big)=1
, so we can just consider
B_{z,\delta}^{-x}
. Let
(l,j)\in[K]^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{(x,l)}\big(N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})\geq 2\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\left[\mathds{1}_{N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})\geq 2}\Big(N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})^{2}-N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})\Big)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\left[N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})^{2}-N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{z,\delta}^{-x}}\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,s,l,j)}\left[N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})-1\right]m_{l,j}(s-x)ds,

where for the last equality we have used (
A.2
). Moreover, for
s\in B_{z,\delta}^{-x}
, since under
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{(x,s,l,j)}
the process has a fixed atom at
(s,j)
and with (
A.5
), we have

\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,s,l,j)}\left[N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x})-1\right]=\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,s,l,j)}\left[N^{j}(B_{z,\delta}^{-x}\backslash\{s\})\right]\lesssim\delta,

which leads to the proof.
∎

The second lemma is on the Lipschitz continuity of the functions
x\mapsto p^{[1]}_{r,k}(x,l)
defined in (
D.5
) at start of the proof of Lemma
D.4

Lemma D.6
.

If the functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
for some
\beta\geq 1
, then for all
r\geq 2
, the functions
x\mapsto p^{[1]}_{r,k}(x,l)
are Lipschitz.

Let
r\geq 2
. First, as in proof of Lemma
D.3
, it is sufficient to show that for
x,y
such that
0\leq y-x\leq A
,
|p^{[1]}_{r,k}(y,l)-p^{[1]}_{r,k}(x,l)|\lesssim|y-x|
. Using the same computations as at start of the proof of Lemma
D.3
and in addition that
a^{r}-b^{r}=(a-b)\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}a^{r-1-k}b^{k}:=(a-b)P_{r}(a,b)
, we find

\displaystyle\Big|p^{[1]}_{r,k}(y,l)-p^{[1]}_{r,k}(x,l)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\bigg[\frac{|\Delta_{y-x}(k,x,l)|\times P_{r}\Big(\lambda_{A+x-y}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(x,l)),\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(x,l))\Big)}{\lambda_{A+x-y}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(x,l))^{r}\lambda_{A}^{k,e}(f^{0}_{k},(x,l))^{r}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{(\nu^{0}_{k})^{r+1}}\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\big[|\Delta_{y-x}(k,x,l)|\big]

where for the last inequality we have used that for
a,b\geq\nu^{0}_{k}
,
P_{r}(a,b)/(a^{r}b^{r})\leq r/(\nu^{0}_{k})^{r+1}
. Then, is it shown in the proof of Lemma
D.3
that
\mathbb{E}_{0}^{(x,l)}\big[|\Delta_{y-x}(k,x,l)|\big]\lesssim|y-x|
and it concludes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

In conclusion, we have the following corollary.

Corollary D.1
.

If the functions
\mathbf{h}^{0}
and
\mathbf{g}^{0}_{2}
are in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
for some
\beta>1
, then, under Conjecture
1
, the functions
\mathbf{g}^{0}_{L}
are also in
C^{\beta}([0,A])
.

E
Technical lemmas

In this section, we state and prove two technical lemmas. First, Lemma
E.1
gives Bernstein-type inequalities that are used in Lemma
C.5
to control the bias term of appearing in Theorem
3.2
. These Bernstein-type inequalities are also used to prove Lemma
E.2
which is the second technical lemma of this section and which shows that the difference between the LAN remainders in the proof of Theorem
3.2
is uniformly controlled.

E.1
Bernstein-type inequalities

The following lemma gives three Bernstein-type inequalities, derived from results of
[
31
]
and
[
53
]
. The first one is for an integral with respect to
dN_{t}-\lambda_{t}(f^{0})dt
and thus the proof exploits the martingale property of this quantity. The second is for an integral with respect to
dN_{t}
whose integrand is centered in expectation, it is based on an approximation of the Hawkes process by i.i.d. sequences (see section 3 of
[
53
]
). The third is a direct combination of the two first.

Lemma E.1
.

Let
N
be a stationary multivariate linear Hawkes process with parameters
f^{0}
such that the functions
h^{0}_{l,k}
are bounded and supported on
[0,A]
. Let
\alpha>0
and with this
\alpha
define
\Omega_{T}
as in Lemma
F.1
. There exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(f^{0},\alpha,K,A)
such that for
T
large enough, for any
f,\bar{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
and
u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{T}\cap\Big\{\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})\big(dN_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt\big)\Big|>u\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{-C\log(T)^{-5}u^{2}}{T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}+u\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

Moreover, there exist an event
\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\subset\Omega_{T}
,
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{T}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{}1
, and a constant
C^{\prime}>0
that depends only on
(f^{0},\alpha,K,A)
such that for
T
large enough, for any
f,\bar{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
and
u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})-\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f_{k})\big]dt\Big|>u\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 4\exp\bigg(\frac{-C^{\prime}\log(T)^{-3}u^{2}}{T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}+u\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

Combining the two previous inequalities, we obtain that there exists a constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for
T
large enough, for any
f,\bar{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\times L_{\infty}^{K^{2}}
and
u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})dN_{t}^{k}-T\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})\big]\Big|>u\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 6\exp\Bigg(\frac{-C^{\prime\prime}\log(T)^{-5}u^{2}}{T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}+u\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}\big)}\Bigg).

We begin by proving the first inequality. Let,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{T}=\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})^{2}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})^{2}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and}\hskip 8.5359pt\hat{B}_{T}=\underset{k\in[K],\hskip 2.84544ptt\in[0,T]}{\sup}|\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})|.

By applying theorem 3.3 of
[
22
]
to the
\mathcal{G}
-martingale

\displaystyle(M_{T}^{0})_{T}:=\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})\big(dN_{t}^{k}-\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})dt\big)\bigg)_{T\geq 0},

we obtain for
u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\big|M_{T}^{0}\big|>u,\hskip 5.69046pt\hat{v}_{T}\leq v\hskip 2.84544pt\text{and}\hskip 2.84544pt\hat{B}_{T}\leq B\Big)\leq 2\exp\Big(\frac{-u^{2}}{v+uB/3}\Big).

First, on the event
\Omega_{T}
, for
T
large enough independently of
f
and
\bar{f}
,

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{T}\leq K^{2}C_{\alpha}^{2}\log(T)^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}.

Secondly, let
r_{T}=K^{3}C_{\alpha}^{3}\log(T)^{3}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}
. With the inequality
(x+y)^{n}\leq 2^{n-1}(x^{n}+y^{n})
and Jensen inequality, we have on
\Omega_{T}
for
T
large enough,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{T}

\displaystyle\leq r_{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})^{2}dt\leq 2r_{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\nu_{k}^{2}+\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{t-A}^{t-}h_{l,k}(t-u)dN_{u}^{l}\Big)^{2}dt

(E.1)

\displaystyle\leq 2r_{T}T\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}+2^{K}r_{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}N^{l}([t-A,t[)\int_{t-A}^{t-}h_{l,k}(t-u)^{2}dN_{u}^{l}dt,

and with Fubini-Tonelli we further obtain on
\Omega_{T}
,

\displaystyle 2^{K}r_{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}N^{l}([t-A,t[)\int_{t-A}^{t-}h_{l,k}(t-u)^{2}dN_{u}^{l}dt

(E.2)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{K}C_{\alpha}r_{T}\log(T)\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{t-A}^{t-}h_{l,k}(t-u)^{2}dN_{u}^{l}dt

\displaystyle=2^{K}C_{\alpha}r_{T}\log(T)\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{-A}^{T}\int_{u+}^{u+A}h_{l,k}(t-u)^{2}dtdN_{u}^{l}

\displaystyle=2^{K}C_{\alpha}r_{T}\log(T)\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{K}N^{l}([-A,T])\|h_{l,k}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{K}C_{\alpha}r_{T}\log(T)(\lceil T/A\rceil+1)\underset{l\in[K],t\in[0,T]}{\sup}N^{l}([t-A,t])\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\|h_{l,k}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{K+1}C_{\alpha}Kr_{T}(\lceil T/A\rceil+1)\log(T)^{2}\|\mathbf{h}\|_{2}^{2}.

Let
C_{0}=cC_{\alpha}^{4}\|f^{0}\|_{\infty}
and
c=2^{K+3}K^{4}/A
, we have proved that for
T
large enough, on
\Omega_{T}
,
\hat{v}_{T}\leq C_{0}\log(T)^{5}T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2},
and thus

\displaystyle\Omega_{T}\subset\big\{\hat{v}_{T}\leq C_{0}\log(T)^{5}T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}\hskip 5.69046pt\text{and}\hskip 5.69046pt\hat{B}_{T}\leq K^{2}C_{\alpha}^{2}\log(T)^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}\big\}.

As a consequence, it proves that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{T}\cap\big\{|M_{T}^{0}|>u\big\}\Big)\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{-\log(T)^{-5}u^{2}}{C_{0}T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}+uK^{2}C_{\alpha}^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}}\bigg),

(E.3)

and letting
C=\max(C_{0},K^{2}C_{\alpha}^{2})^{-1}
, we have proved the first inequality.

For the second inequality, we adapt the proof of proposition 3 of
[
31
]
to obtain a Bernstein-type inequality on a certain event
\Omega_{T}^{\prime}=\Omega_{T}^{1}\cap\Omega_{T}^{2}
defined just after. For a real Borel set
A
, we denote by
N_{|A}
the random measure
N
restricted to
A
:
N_{|A}(B)=N(B\cap A)
. To begin with,

\displaystyle Z_{t}:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})-\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f_{k})].

depends only on
N_{|[t-A,t[}
and can be written as
Z_{0}\circ\mathfrak{S}_{t}(N)
with
\mathfrak{S}_{t}
the time shift operator (it means that
Z_{t}
depends on
N_{|[t-A,t[}
as
Z_{0}
depends on
N_{|[-A,0[}
). Let
c>0
fixed later,
d_{T}=\lfloor cT/\log(T)\rfloor
and
x=x_{T}=T/2d_{T}
. Note that
x
is of order
\log(T)
and so for
T
large enough
x>A
. We split

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})-\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f_{k})]dt\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\sum_{q=0}^{d_{T}-1}\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\hskip-5.69046ptZ_{0}\circ\mathfrak{S}_{t}(N)dt\Big|+\Big|\sum_{q=0}^{d_{T}-1}\int_{2qx+x}^{2qx+2x}\hskip-5.69046ptZ_{0}\circ\mathfrak{S}_{t}(N)dt\big)\Big|=:\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2},

and we treat each term separately but using a similar argument. We first study
\Delta_{1}
. There exists a sequence
M^{x,1}=(M^{x,1}_{q})_{q}
of i.i.d. point processes such that for all
q
,
M^{x,1}_{q}
has the same distribution as
N_{|[2qx-A,2qx+x]}
. Moreover, there exists
c>0
small enough (in the definition of
x
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists q\leq d_{T}-1,\,M^{x,1}_{q}\neq N_{|[2qx-A,2qx+x]}\Big)=o(1).

(E.4)

Hence, if
\Omega_{1,T}=\Omega_{T}\cap\{\forall q\leq d_{T}-1,\,M^{x,1}_{q}=N_{|[2qx-A,2qx+x]}\}
then
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1,T}^{c})=o(1)
; see section 3 of
[
53
]
for details on the construction of
(M_{q}^{x,1})_{q}
and for a proof of (
E.4
). On
\Omega_{1,T}
, since
\sup_{t\in[0;T]}N([t-A,t[)\leq KC_{\alpha}\log T
, we also have
\max_{q}\sup_{t\in[0;T]}M_{q}^{x,1}([t-A,t[)\leq KC_{\alpha}\log T
. Next, let
F_{q}=\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}Z_{0}\circ\mathfrak{S}_{t}(M^{x,1}_{q})dt
, the variables
(F_{q})_{q}
are independent and centered so they form a martingale difference sequence with respect to their natural filtration denoted by
\mathfrak{F}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{1}\bigcap\Big\{\Big|\sum_{q=0}^{d_{T}-1}\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}Z_{0}\circ\mathfrak{S}_{t}(N)dt\Big|>\frac{u}{2}\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{M^{x,1}}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{1}\bigcap\Big\{\Big|\sum_{q=0}^{d_{T}-1}F_{q}\Big|>\frac{u}{2}\Big\}\bigg).

For
T
large enough (independently of
f
and
\bar{f}
), on the event
\Omega_{T}^{1}
,

\displaystyle|F_{q}|

\displaystyle\leq x\hskip 2.84544pt\underset{t\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}|Z_{0}\circ\mathfrak{S}_{t}(N)|

\displaystyle\leq x\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}\Big(\underset{t\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}\big(1+M^{x,1}_{q}([t-A,t[)\big)^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[(1+N([0,A]))^{2}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2K^{2}C_{\alpha}^{2}\log(T)^{2}x\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}=\frac{4K^{2}C_{\alpha}^{2}}{c}\log(T)^{3}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}.

Then, by independence
\mathbb{E}_{M^{x,1}}[F_{q}^{2}|\mathfrak{F}_{q-1}]=\mathbb{E}_{M^{x,1}_{q}}[F_{q}^{2}]
. As
M^{x,1}_{q}
is equal in distribution to
N_{|[2qx-A,2qx+x]}
, we also have
\mathbb{E}_{M^{x,1}_{q}}[F_{q}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{0}[F_{q}^{2}]
. By Jensen inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[F_{q}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)-\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f)]dt\Big)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{K-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\Big(\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)-\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f)]dt\Big)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{K}x\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f})^{2}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f)]^{2}dt\bigg].

Let
k\in[K]
, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f)]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f})^{2}]\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(f)^{2}]\leq K\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[(1+N([0,A]))^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(f)^{2}].

In the same way as in the proof of (
B.1
), one can show that there exists a finite positive constant
C_{2}^{\prime}
, independent of
f
, such that
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)^{2}]\leq C_{2}^{\prime}\|f\|_{2}^{2}
. Hence, for some
c(K)>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[F_{q}^{2}]\leq c(K)x^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}+2^{K}x\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f})^{2}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)^{2}dt\bigg].

Next, using similar computations to those used in (
E.2
) (in particular Fubini theorem to switch
dN_{u}
and
dt
), we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f})^{2}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)^{2}dt\bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\underset{l\in[K],t^{\prime}\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}\lambda_{t^{\prime}}^{l}(\bar{f})^{2}\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)^{2}dt\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\underset{l\in[K],t^{\prime}\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}(1+N^{l}([t^{\prime}-A,t^{\prime}])^{2}\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\lambda_{t}^{k}(f)^{2}dt\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2x\nu_{k}^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\underset{l\in[K],t^{\prime}\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}(1+N^{l}([t^{\prime}-A,t^{\prime}])^{2}\big]

\displaystyle+2^{K}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\underset{t^{\prime}\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}(1+N([t^{\prime}-A,t^{\prime}])^{2}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{2qx}^{2qx+x}\hskip-2.84544pt\Big(\int_{t-A}^{t-}\hskip-5.69046pth_{l,k}(A-u)dN_{u}^{l}\Big)^{2}\hskip-4.26773ptdt\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2x\nu_{k}^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\underset{l\in[K],t^{\prime}\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}(1+N^{l}([t^{\prime}-A,t^{\prime}])^{2}\big]

\displaystyle+2^{K}\|h_{k}\|^{2}_{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\underset{t^{\prime}\in[2qx,2qx+x]}{\sup}(1+N([t^{\prime}-A,t^{\prime}])^{3}N([2qx-A,2qx+x])\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2x\|f\|_{2}^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\underset{t^{\prime}\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t^{\prime}-A,t^{\prime}])^{2}\big]

\displaystyle+2^{K}\|f\|_{2}^{2}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\underset{t^{\prime}\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t^{\prime}-A,t^{\prime}])^{3}N([2qx-A,2qx+x])\big].

Finally, let
p\geq 0
, note that by Lemma
F.1
, we have for
T
large enough

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t-A,t[))^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t-A,t[))^{p}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{T}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t-A,t[))^{p}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{T}^{c}}\Big]\leq 2K^{p}C_{\alpha}^{p}\log(T)^{p}.

Putting all together, we obtain that for
T
large enough

\displaystyle\sum_{q=0}^{d_{T}-1}\mathbb{E}_{0}[F_{q}^{2}|\mathfrak{F}_{q-1}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}\bigg(d_{T}x^{2}+d_{T}x\log(T)^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t-A,t]))^{2}\sum_{q=0}^{d_{T}-1}N([2qx-A,2qx+x])\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}\bigg(T\log(T)+T\log(T)^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t-A,t]))^{2}N([-A,T])\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}\bigg(T\log(T)^{2}+T\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}(1+N([t-A,t]))^{3}\Big]\bigg)\lesssim T\log(T)^{3}\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore, again by theorem 3.3 of
[
22
]
(see also equation (1.1) of this article for a formulation in discrete time), for some constant finite constant
C^{\prime}>0
and for
T
large enough, both independent of
f
and
\bar{f}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{M^{x,1}}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{1}\cap\Big\{\Big|\sum_{q=0}^{d_{T}-1}F_{q}\Big|>\frac{u}{2}\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{-C^{\prime}\log(T)^{-3}u^{2}}{T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}+u\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

It remains to study the term
\Delta_{2}
. To do so, we proceed as for
\Delta_{1}
but with this time a sequence
(M^{x,2}_{q})_{q}
such that for all
q
,
M^{x,2}_{q}
that has the same distribution as
N_{|[2qx+x-A,2qx+2x]}
and we define
\Omega_{2,T}:=\Omega_{T}\cap\{\forall q\leq d_{T}-1,\,M^{x,2}_{q}=N_{|[2qx+x-A,2qx+2x]}\}
. Then, everything done for
\Delta_{1}
holds similarly for
\Delta_{2}
and, recalling that we have set
\Omega_{T}^{\prime}=\Omega_{T}^{1}\cap\Omega_{T}^{2}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\lambda_{t}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k})-\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(\bar{f}_{k})\lambda_{A}^{k}(f_{k})]dt\big)\Big|>u\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 4\exp\bigg(\frac{-C^{\prime}\log(T)^{-3}u^{2}}{T\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f\|_{2}^{2}+u\|\bar{f}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}}\bigg).

This ends the proof of the second inequality.
∎

E.2
Control of the remainders in the LAN expansions for the proof of Theorem
3.2

The following lemma is used in the proof of Theorem
3.2
to get rid of remainder terms coming from the LAN expansions. The proof relies on a chaining argument from
[
60
]
for variables satisfying Bernstein-type inequalities.

Lemma E.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.2
, the remainder term
R_{T,\varphi}
defined in the proof of Theorem
3.2
verifies

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}|R_{T,\varphi}(f)-R_{T,\varphi}(f_{u,j})|=o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(1)

First, to shorten some computations, given
H=(H_{t}^{k})_{t,k}
a marked, stationary and predictable process having a second moment, we define the process
H\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}
by

\displaystyle H\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}H_{t}^{k}dN_{t}^{k}-T\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}[H_{A}^{k}\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})].

Moreover, a process
\big(\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f)\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{\prime})/\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{\prime\prime})\big)_{t,k}
will be denoted
\tilde{\lambda}(f)\tilde{\lambda}(f^{\prime})/\tilde{\lambda}(f^{\prime\prime})
. Let
\Omega_{T}^{\prime}
be the event defined in Lemma
E.1
, as
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{T}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{}1
, it is sufficient to prove that for all
x>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\Big(\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}|R_{T,\varphi}(f)-R_{T,\varphi}(f_{u,j})|>x\Big)\rightarrow 0,

(E.5)

where
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}
is the conditional probability given
\Omega_{T}^{\prime}
:
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}(.)=\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap.)/\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{T}^{\prime})
. Now we recall that for
f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}
,

\displaystyle R_{T,\varphi}(f)=T\Delta Q_{T}(f-f^{0})+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}V\Big(\frac{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big)\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}+\tilde{R}_{T,\varphi}(f),

(E.6)

\displaystyle\Delta Q_{T}(f-f^{0})=\frac{1}{2}\|f-f^{0}\|_{L}^{2}-\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})^{2}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})},

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{T,\varphi}(f)=\sqrt{T}W_{T}\big(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})\big)-\frac{T}{2}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})\|_{L}^{2}-T\langle\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))\rangle_{L},

with
V(x)=\log(1+x)-x+x^{2}/2=x^{2}R(x)
for
x>-1
. We will study separately the difference between the evaluation in
f_{u,j}
and in
f
of each term appearing in (
E.6
) for some integer
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and some
f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)
. Next, for each term, we will obtain a uniform control of the difference over
f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)
and then over
\mathcal{A}_{T}
. We begin with the difference
T(\Delta Q_{T}(f_{u,j}-f^{0})-\Delta Q_{T}(f-f^{0}))
.

Difference
\mathit{T|\Delta Q_{T}(f_{u,j}-f^{0})-\Delta Q_{T}(f-f^{0})}|
. Let
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and let
f=(\nu,\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)
, set as before
\tilde{f}=(\nu,\tilde{\mathbf{h}})
and
\tilde{f}\varphi^{0}=(\nu,\tilde{\mathbf{h}}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})
. We first use the linearization (
C.11
) (justified by lemma
C.7
) to rewrite the difference:

\displaystyle T\big(\Delta Q_{T}(f_{u,j}-f^{0})-\Delta Q_{T}(f-f^{0})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{u^{2}}{2T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\ -\frac{u}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt-\frac{u}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt-\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}.

(E.7)

We study in detail the first term and the second term on the right-hand side of (
E.2
). Then, we will show that the third can treated similarly to the second term. Finally, we will treat the last three terms with simple inequalities on
\omega_{\varphi}
and using what we did for the second and third terms. Note that in the case where
\varphi
is the identity function, the last four terms are equal to
0
and for two first terms, the functions
\bar{\varphi}^{0}_{l,k}
are constant and equal to
1
.

The first term on the right-hand side of (
E.2
) is independent of
f
. By assumption we have for some
c>0
independent of
j
,
\|\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\|_{\infty}<c
. Moreover, by the equivalence of norms and since an orthogonal projection is norm-decreasing, we find

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}\lesssim\|\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}

Hence, with the third Bernstein inequality of Lemma
E.1
, we obtain that for some
B_{1}>0
, for any
x>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\Big|\frac{1}{2T}\frac{\lambda(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x\Big\}\bigg)\leq 6\exp\bigg(\frac{-B_{1}Tx^{2}}{\log(T)^{5}\big(c^{2}\|\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}^{2}+xc^{2}\big)}\bigg).

This upper bound does not depend on
j
so,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\bigg(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\Big|\frac{1}{2T}\frac{\lambda(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 6J_{T}\exp\bigg(\frac{-B_{1}Tx^{2}}{\log(T)^{5}\big(c^{2}\|\tilde{\psi}^{0}_{L,\varphi}\|_{L,\varphi}^{2}+xc^{2}\big)}\bigg)=o(1).

It shows that the first term on the right-hand side of (
E.2
) is a
o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(1)
uniformly in
j
.

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
E.2
), we set

\displaystyle H_{2}(\tilde{f},T):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}},

and so we have to show that

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\hskip 1.42271pt\underset{\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\Big|H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}}0,

(E.8)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)
is such that
\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)=\{\varphi(\tilde{f}),\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)\}
. To do so, we first fix
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and we study the increments of the process
\big(H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\big)_{\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)}
(which is a kind of empirical process) in order to conclude with a chaining argument. By the third inequality of lemma
E.1
and with the previous remarks on the sup norm and the
L_{2}
norm of
\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}
, there exists
B_{2}>0
such that for any
\tilde{f},\tilde{f}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)
and
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\Big|\big(H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)-H_{2}(\tilde{f}^{\prime}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\big)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E})_{T}\big|>x\Big\}\Big)

(E.9)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\Big|H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime},T)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E})_{T}\big|>x\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\bigg(\frac{-B_{2}\log(T)^{-5}x^{2}}{\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}+xT^{-1/2}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}\bigg)\lesssim\exp\bigg(\frac{-B_{2}\log(T)^{-5}x^{2}}{\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}+x\sqrt{j}T^{-1/2}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\bigg),

where the last inequality comes from (
3.3
). With inequality (
E.9
), we can apply Theorem 1.2.7 of
[
60
]
(more precisely the penultimate inequality in the proof of this Theorem), which combined with inequality 1.49 again from
[
60
]
gives the following bound (
E.10
) on the deviation of the supremum around some
\tilde{f}^{j}_{*}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)
. Let
d_{\|.\|_{2}}
be the distance induced by
\|.\|_{2}
. There exists a universal constant
\bar{L}>0
such that for any
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\Bigg(\underset{\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\Big|\Big(H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)-H_{2}(\tilde{f}^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\Big)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x\bigg)

(E.10)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{L}\exp\Bigg(\frac{-\bar{L}^{-1}B_{2}\log(T)^{-5}x}{E_{1}(T,j)+E_{2}(T,j)}\Bigg)

with

\displaystyle E_{1}(T,j)=\sqrt{j}T^{-1/2}\inf\underset{\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\sum_{n\geq 0}2^{n}d_{\|.\|_{2}}(\tilde{f},\tilde{A}_{n})

\displaystyle E_{2}(T,j)=\inf\underset{\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\sum_{n\geq 0}2^{n/2}d_{\|.\|_{2}}(\tilde{f},\tilde{A}_{n})

and where the infimum is each time taken on the sequences
(\tilde{A}_{n})_{n}
of subsets of
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)
such that
Card(\tilde{A}_{0})=1
and
Card(\tilde{A}_{n})\leq 2^{2^{n}}
for
n\geq 1
. We first bound the term
E_{1}(T,j)
. Let
\tilde{f}_{c}=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{c}^{T}B_{j}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)
, by definition we have

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)\subset\big\{\tilde{f}\in[0,+\infty[^{K}\times\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(j)^{K^{2}};\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}_{c}\|_{2}\leq 2M\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}\big\}:=\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)^{ext}.

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)^{ext}
is a
\|.\|_{2}
-ball in
[0,+\infty[^{K}\times\mathcal{\tilde{H}}(j)^{K^{2}}
and an internal covering of
\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)^{ext}
is an external covering of
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)
. So, with (
3.2
), there exists
c>0
such that any
r>0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(r,\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j),\|.\|_{2})\leq\mathcal{N}\big(r/2,\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)^{ext},\|.\|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}\big(r/2,B_{\|.\|}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{c},2cM\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}),\|.\|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big(1,\frac{12cM\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}}{r}\Big)^{K^{2}j},

where
\|.\|
is the usual euclidean norm on
\mathbb{R}^{K^{2}j}
. Let
c^{\prime}=12cM
, we choose
r_{n,j,T}=c^{\prime}\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}2^{-2^{n}/(K^{2}j)}
so that the set of centering points of the covering of
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)
with radius
r_{n,j,T}
, denoted
\tilde{A}_{n,T}
, verifies
Card(\tilde{A}_{n,T})\leq 2^{2^{n}}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle E_{1}(T,j)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{j}T^{-1/2}\sum_{n\geq 0}2^{n}r_{n,j,T}\leq K^{2}c^{\prime}\log(T)T^{-1/2}\varepsilon_{T}J_{T}^{3/2}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{2^{n}}{K^{2}j}2^{-2^{n}/(K^{2}j)}.

where for the last inequality we have used that
j\leq J_{T}
. Let
n_{j}:=\lceil\log(3K^{2}j)/\log(2)\rceil
, if
n\geq n_{j}
then
2^{n}/(K^{2}j)\geq 3
. Moreover, the map
x\mapsto x2^{-x}
is bounded by
1
on
[0,+\infty[
and is decreasing on
[2,+\infty[
. Whence,

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{2^{n}}{K^{2}j}2^{-\frac{2^{n}}{K^{2}j}}\leq n_{j}+\sum_{n\geq n_{j}}\frac{2^{n}}{K^{2}j}2^{-\frac{2^{n}}{K^{2}j}}\leq n_{j}+\int_{n_{j}-1}^{+\infty}\frac{2^{x}}{K^{2}j}2^{-\frac{2^{x}}{K^{2}j}}dx\leq n_{j}+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{e^{-u\log(2)}}{\log(2)}du,

and the last inequality comes with the change of variable
u=2^{x}/(K^{2}j)
. So, for
T
large enough,
\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{2^{n}}{K^{2}j}2^{-\frac{2^{n}}{K^{2}j}}\leq 2\log(J_{T})
and thus,
E_{1}(T,j)\lesssim\log(T)^{2}T^{-1/2}\varepsilon_{T}J_{T}^{3/2}
. The last bound being independent of
j\leq J_{T}
, we even have

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\hskip 2.84544ptE_{1}(T,j)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)^{2}T^{-1/2}\varepsilon_{T}J_{T}^{3/2}\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T},

because by
(P1)
,
\log(T)J_{T}\lesssim T\varepsilon_{T}^{2}
and by (
3.14
),
\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}=o(1)
. Now, for the second term appearing in (
E.10
), namely
E_{2}(T,j)
, using Dudley inequality (see again
[
60
]
), we find similarly that

E_{2}(T,j)\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}
.
Therefore, we have proved that for some finite positive constant
L^{\prime}
, we have for any
j\leq J_{T}
and
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\Bigg(\underset{\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\Big|\Big(H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)-H_{2}(\tilde{f}^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\Big)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x\bigg)\leq L^{\prime}\exp\bigg(\frac{-L^{\prime}x}{\log(T)^{6}\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}}\bigg).

We deduce that over
\mathcal{A}_{T}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\Bigg(\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}}{\sup}\Big|H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x\bigg)

(E.11)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\Bigg(\underset{\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\Big|\Big(H_{2}(\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)-H_{2}(\tilde{f}^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\Big)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x/2\bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}P_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\Bigg(\Big|H_{2}(\tilde{f}^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x/2\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim J_{T}L^{\prime}\exp\bigg(\frac{-L^{\prime}x/2}{\log(T)^{6}\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}}\bigg)+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}P_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\Bigg(\Big|H_{2}(\tilde{f}^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x/2\bigg).

For the first term on the right hand side of (
E.11
), one can check that by assumption (
3.14
), it goes to
0
as
T\rightarrow+\infty
. For the second term on the right hand side of (
E.11
), recall that by definition of
\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)
,
\|f^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}
and with (
3.3
) we also have
\|f^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}=o(1)
. Whence, for some
B_{2}^{\prime}>0
and
T
large enough, we have for
x>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}P_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\bigg(\Big|H_{2}(\tilde{f}^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0},T)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x/2\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}12\exp\bigg(-\frac{B_{2}^{\prime}\log(T)^{-5}Tx^{2}}{T\|f^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}+x\sqrt{T}\|f^{j}_{*}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{\infty}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 12J_{T}\exp\bigg(-\frac{B_{2}^{\prime}\log(T)^{-5}x^{2}}{\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}+xT^{-1/2}}\bigg)=o(1).

which terminates to prove (
E.8
).

So far, we have treated the two first terms on the right-hand side of (
E.2
), it remains to deal with the following four terms in (
E.2
). These four terms involve the remainder
\omega_{\varphi}
and we first recall some facts and prove useful inequalities on it. These inequalities will also be useful to study
\tilde{R}_{T,\varphi}(f_{u,j})-\tilde{R}_{T,\varphi}(f)
later in the proof. First, recall that

\displaystyle\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})=\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})-(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0},

and that by Lemma
C.7
,
\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})\|_{1}\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}
on
\mathcal{A}_{T}
. Take
f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{T}
, since
\varphi
is Lipschitz and the functions
\bar{\varphi}^{0}
are bounded, we have for any
p\in[1,+\infty]

\displaystyle\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{\prime})\|_{p}\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{\prime}\|_{p},

(E.12)

and thus

\displaystyle\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})\|_{p}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}.

(E.13)

We can even be more precise:

\displaystyle\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})

\displaystyle=\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}

(E.14)

\displaystyle=(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}).\big(\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{u\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}}{\sqrt{T}}.\big(\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})\big).

and for all
(l,k)\in[K]^{2}
,
\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}})_{l,k}
is a function in the bracket
[\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j,l,k},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{l,k}]
. We will use throughout the proof, without recalling it, that by assumption
\sup\{\|\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\|_{\infty},j\geq 1\}<+\infty
. Finally, recall (
C.9
) which gives that for some
D^{\prime}>0

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T}\subset\big\{f:\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq D^{\prime}\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}\big\},

and
\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}\rightarrow 0
by assumption. Now, we are ready to study individually the last four terms of (
E.2
).

For the third term, define for
\tilde{f}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}(j)

\displaystyle H_{3}(\tilde{f},T):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))}{\tilde{\lambda}(f^{0})^{2}}

Using the third inequality of lemma
E.1
and (
E.12
), we find that for some
B_{3},B_{3}^{\prime}>0
(independent of
f,f^{\prime},T
and
x
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\Omega_{T}^{\prime}\cap\Big\{\Big|\big(H_{3}(\tilde{f},T)-H_{3}(\tilde{f}^{\prime},T)\big)\bullet(N-\mathbb{E})_{T}\big|>x\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-T\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{A}^{k}(\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi})\tilde{\lambda}_{A}^{k}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{\prime}))}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg]\Big|>\sqrt{T}x\Big\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 6\exp\bigg(\frac{-B_{3}x^{2}}{\log(T)^{5}\big(\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}+xT^{-1/2}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{\prime})\|_{\infty}\big)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 6\exp\bigg(\frac{-B_{3}^{\prime}x^{2}}{\log(T)^{5}\big(\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}\|_{2}^{2}+xT^{-1/2}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\big)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 6\exp\bigg(\frac{-B_{3}\log(T)^{-5}x^{2}}{\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}+x\sqrt{j}T^{-1/2}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\bigg).

Using the same arguments as for
H_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\Omega_{T}^{\prime}}\bigg(\hskip 1.42271pt\underset{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{T}}{\sup}\Big|H_{3}(\tilde{f})\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|>x\bigg)\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{}0.

For the fourth, fifth and sixth terms, we could again study the increment of the associated process and conclude with a chaining argument. But thanks to (
E.14
) and (
C.9
), we can be more concise (and rougher). For the fourth term, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\bigg|

(E.15)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}((\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}^{k}_{t}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j,k})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{k}))}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{t}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle+T\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\Big|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{A}^{k}((\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{A}^{k}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})_{k}-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})_{k})}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big|\bigg]

Now, using in order Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the equivalence between the LAN norm and the L2 norm, (
E.14
) and that by assumption
\varphi^{\prime}
is Lipschitz on
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}_{T}(j)
for
T
large enough, we obtain the following bound on the second term of the right hand side of (
E.15
):

\displaystyle T\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|(\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k}\|_{L}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j,k})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{k})\|_{L}

(E.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim T\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|(\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k}\|_{2}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j,k})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{k})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)T\varepsilon_{T}\Big\|\frac{u\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}}{\sqrt{T}}\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}\|\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}\|\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}\big(\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}=o(1).

Next, for the first term on the right hand side of (
E.15
), with similar arguments (in particular (
E.14
)) we find that:

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}((\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j,k})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{k}))}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{t}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}((|\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}|).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j,k})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{k})|)}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{t}^{k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}((|\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}|).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(0,|\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}))|)}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{t}^{k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}((|\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}|).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}\big(0,|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}|+|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}|\big)\big)}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{t}^{k}.

Moreover, similarly to (
E.16
), we have on the event
\Omega_{T}

\displaystyle T\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}((|\tilde{f}_{k}-\tilde{f}^{0}_{k}|).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}_{k})\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}\big(0,|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}|+|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}|\big)}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg]\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}.

Whence, on the event
\Omega_{T}
, we have for the fourth term:

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\bigg|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}|).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(0,|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}|+|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}|)}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\bigg|+\log(T)^{2}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}.

(E.17)

Using triangular inequality and what we did for the second and third term of (
E.2
), one can obtain a uniform control over
\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)
of the first term on the right hand of side of (
E.2
). We can then as before deduce that

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\bigg|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\bigg|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0.

The fifth term can be treated as the fourth one.

The sixth term can be directly bounded. Indeed,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})_{k}-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})_{k})^{2}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{T}^{k}+T\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{A}^{k}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})_{k}-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})_{k})^{2}}{\lambda_{A}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg].

(E.18)

Using (
E.14
) and (
C.9
), we find that on the event
\Omega_{T}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})_{k}-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})_{k})^{2}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{T}^{k}

\displaystyle\lesssim N([0,T])\log(T)^{2}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N([0,T])}{T}\log(T)^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\big(\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)^{4}J_{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{}0.

In the same way, the expectation on the right hand side of (
E.2
), can be uniformly bounded independently of
f
and
j
by some sequence
u_{T}\rightarrow 0
. Thus, it proves that

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\Big|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\Big|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0.

Coming back to (
E.6
), it remains to study the term involving the function
V
and the term
\tilde{R}_{T,\varphi}
.

Term involving function
\mathit{V}
:

We recall that for
x>-1
,
V(x)=\log(1+x)-x+x^{2}/2
,
V^{\prime}(x)=x^{2}/(1+x)
and
V^{\prime\prime}(x)=(x^{2}+2x)/(1+x)^{2}
. We first fix
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
and
f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)
. To begin, note that by (
C.9
), on the event
\Omega_{T}
, for any
t\in[0,T]
and
k\in[K]
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big|\lesssim\log(T)\|f-f^{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}=o(1),

(E.19)

and

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)\|f_{u,j}-f\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}=o(1).

(E.20)

Then, by Taylor-Lagrange expansion, for all
k\in[K]
and
t\geq 0
, there exists a random variable

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}^{k}(f)\in\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})},\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg],

such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}V\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg)-V\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg)\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}V^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big)\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f_{k})^{2}}{2\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}V^{\prime\prime}\big(\Gamma_{t}^{k}(f)\big)\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}.

(E.21)

For the first term on the right hand side of (
E.2
), with the two previous remarks (
E.19
) and (
E.20
) and the fact that for
|x|<1/2
,
|V^{\prime}(x)|\leq 2x^{2}
, we have on the event
\Omega_{T}
for
T
large enough

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}V^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big)\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})^{2}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}dN_{t}^{k}\lesssim\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(f-f^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}+\log(T)\sqrt{T}\|f-f^{0}\|_{L}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(f-f^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}+\log(T)^{3}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2},

and by assumption
\log(T)^{3}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}\rightarrow 0
. Furthermore, linearizing again by Lemma
C.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(f-f^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}=\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}

(E.22)

\displaystyle+\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}))^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}

\displaystyle+2\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}((\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0})\tilde{\lambda}(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}))}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}.

Then, because
\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}}{\sup}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{\infty}\leq C
and
\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}}{\sup}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{\infty}\leq C
for some
C>0
, these three terms on the right hand side of (
E.22
) can be treated in the same way as the second and the third terms of (
E.2
). Thus, we have that

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\frac{\log(T)}{\sqrt{T}}\bigg|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(f-f^{0})^{2}}{\lambda(f^{0})^{2}}\bullet(N-\mathbb{E}_{0})_{T}\bigg|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0,

and therefore

\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f_{k})}{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}V^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{k}-f^{0}_{k})}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\Big)dN_{t}^{k}\bigg|=o_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}(1).

Now, for the second term on the right hand side of (
E.2
), using (
E.19
), (
E.20
) and that
|V^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq 4(x^{2}+2|x|)
for
|x|\leq 1/2
, we can bound it on the event
\Omega_{T}
(for
T
large enough) in the following way,

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f_{k})^{2}}{2\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}V^{\prime\prime}\big(\Gamma_{t}^{k}(f)\big)\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim N([0,T])\log(T)^{3}\|f_{u,j}-f\|_{\infty}^{2}\times\underset{t\in[0,T],k\in[K]}{\sup}\big|V^{\prime\prime}\big(\Gamma_{t}^{k}(f)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N([0,T])\log(T)^{3}}{T}\times\underset{t\in[0,T],k\in[K]}{\sup}|\Gamma_{t}^{k}(f)|^{2}+2|\Gamma_{t}^{k}(f)|\lesssim\log(T)^{5}\sqrt{J_{T}}\varepsilon_{T}=o(1).

Whence,

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{t}^{k}(f_{u,j,k}-f_{k})^{2}}{2\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})^{2}}V^{\prime\prime}\big(\Gamma_{t}^{k}(f)\big)\frac{dN_{t}^{k}}{\lambda_{t}^{k}(f^{0}_{k})}\bigg|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0.

Lastly we have to study the difference of the terms defined by
R_{T,\varphi}
.

Difference

\mathit{\tilde{R}_{T,\varphi}(f_{u,j})-\tilde{R}_{T,\varphi}(f)}
. Let
j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}
(in particular
j\leq J_{T}
) and
f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)
. First, recall that by Lemma
C.7
we have
\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{1}^{1/2}+\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{1}^{1/2}\lesssim\log(T)\varepsilon_{T}
. Moreover, by (
C.9
),
\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{\infty}^{1/2}+\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{\infty}^{1/2}
can be bounded by a independently of
j
and
\tilde{f}
. These two remarks, combined with as before (
E.14
) and the equivalence of norms, give

\displaystyle T\Big|\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{L}^{2}-\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{L}^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq T\big(\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{L}+\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{L}\big)\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{L}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\big(\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{2}+\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{2}\big)\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\big(\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{1}^{1/2}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{\infty}^{1/2}+\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{1}^{1/2}\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{\infty}^{1/2}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}\times\|\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0})\|_{2}\lesssim\log(T)\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}=o(1),

by assumption. So,
\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}T\Big|\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})\|_{L}^{2}-\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{L}^{2}\Big|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{}0
. Secondly, these arguments also give

\displaystyle T\big|\langle\tilde{f}_{u,j}-\tilde{f}^{0},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j}))\rangle_{L}-\langle\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}))\rangle_{L}\big|

\displaystyle\leq T\big|\langle\tilde{f}_{u,j}-\tilde{f},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j}))\rangle_{L}\big|+T\big|\langle\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}))\rangle_{L}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{T}\big|\langle\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j}))\rangle_{L}\big|+T\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0}\|_{2}\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{T}\big|\langle\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j}))\rangle_{L}\big|+\log(T)^{2}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}.

Now, for
k\in[K]
let
p_{k,n}=\mathbb{P}_{0}(N^{k}([0,A])=n)
. There exists
v>0
such that for any
k\in[K]
the variable
vN^{k}([0,A])
has exponential moments (see proposition 3 of
[
31
]
for instance). Therefore,
p_{k,n}=o(e^{-vn})
and thus for any
a,b>0
the series of general term
n^{a}p_{k,n}^{b}
converges. Moreover, since
\mathbb{E}_{0}[\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u}))|]\lesssim\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})\|_{1}\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\langle\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u}))\rangle_{L}\big|\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\big(1+N([0,A])\big)\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[N([0,A])\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)\Big].

Let
0<\delta<1
be as in Theorem
3.2
, let
k\in[K]
, by Hölder inequality (with
p=1/(1-\delta)
and
q=1/\delta
), we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[N([0,A])\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[N([0,A])\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)^{\delta}\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)^{1-\delta}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})\|_{\infty}^{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[N([0,A])^{1+\delta}\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)^{1-\delta}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[N([0,A])^{1+\delta}\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)^{1-\delta}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 0}n^{1+\delta}\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\mathds{1}_{N^{k}([0,A])=n}\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)^{1-\delta}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geq 0}n^{1+\delta}p_{k,n}^{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{0}\big[\lambda_{A}^{k}(0,|\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u})|)\big]^{1-\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}\Big)^{1-\delta}\sum_{n\geq 0}n^{1+\delta}p_{k,n}^{1/\delta}\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\varepsilon_{T}^{2(1-\delta)}.

Consequently,
\sqrt{T}\big|\langle\tilde{\psi}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u}))\rangle_{L}\big|\lesssim\log(T)^{2}\sqrt{T}\varepsilon_{T}^{2(1-\delta)}=o(1)
. So, it proves that

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\max}\underset{f\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}T\big|\langle\tilde{f}_{u,j}-\tilde{f}^{0},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j}))\rangle_{L}-\langle\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}^{0},(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}))\rangle_{L}\big|=o(1).

Thirdly, we have to show that

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\sup}\hskip 1.42271pt\underset{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\sqrt{T}\big|W_{T}\big(0,\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{h})\big)\big|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0.

(E.23)

To do so, we begin by rewriting in a different way (
E.14
). Using a Taylor expansion at order 3 around
\tilde{h}
(we can by assumption
(A’)
) we find

\displaystyle\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\omega_{\varphi}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})

\displaystyle=\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j})-\varphi(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}).\boldsymbol{\bar{\varphi}}^{0}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}).(\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}))+\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}})^{2}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})+\frac{1}{6}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}})^{3}.\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))

\displaystyle=\frac{-u\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}}{\sqrt{T}}.(\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}))+\frac{u^{2}(\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi})^{2}}{2T}.\varphi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\frac{u^{3}(\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi})^{3}}{6T^{3/2}}.\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}})).

and as before
\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}})
is a function in the bracket
[\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}]
. Whence, to prove (
E.23
), it is enough to prove that

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\sup}\hskip 1.42271pt\underset{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\big|W_{T}\big(0,\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi}.(\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})-\varphi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}^{0}))\big)\big|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0,

(E.24)

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\sup}\hskip 1.42271pt\underset{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}W_{T}\big(0,(\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi})^{2}.\varphi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}})\big)\big|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0,

(E.25)

\displaystyle\underset{j\in\mathcal{J}_{T}}{\sup}\hskip 1.42271pt\underset{\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{T}(j)}{\sup}\frac{1}{T}\big|W_{T}\big(0,(\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi})^{3}.\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))\big)\big|\xrightarrow[T\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0.

(E.26)

(
E.24
) and (
E.25
) can be proved with the same chaining argument and the similar computations to those used for the second term of (
E.2
) (for the Bernstein inequality, use the second inequality of lemma
E.1
instead of the third one and then recall that
\varphi^{\prime}
and
\varphi^{\prime\prime}
are Lipschitz on
\varphi^{-1}(I_{0}(G))
). For (
E.26
), it can be directly uniformly bounded on the event
\Omega_{T}
, independently of
f
and
j
in the following way. Recall that
\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}
is bounded on
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{T}
and we obtain on the event
\Omega_{T}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\big|W_{T}\big(0,(\mathbf{g}^{0,j}_{L,\varphi})^{3}.\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{u,j},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}))\big)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{T^{3/2}}\big(\log(T)N([0,T])+\log(T)^{2}T\big)\lesssim\frac{\log(T)^{2}}{\sqrt{T}}.

Therefore, (
E.26
) is also proved. It concludes the proof of Lemma
E.2
.
∎

F
Control of the number of points of the process

We recall here lemma A.1 of
[
58
]
on the number of points of the process.

Lemma F.1
.

Let
N
be a stationary,
K
-multivariate and non-linear ReLu Hawkes process with parameters
f^{0}
such that functions
h^{0}_{l,k}
are supported on a known and bounded interval
[0,A]
. For any
Q>0
and
\alpha>0
, there exists a constant
C_{\alpha}
, depending only on
f^{0}
such that for
T>0
, the set

\displaystyle\Omega_{T}=\bigg\{\underset{l\in[K]}{\max}\hskip 2.84544pt\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}N^{l}\big([t-A,t]\big)\leq C_{\alpha}\log(T)\bigg\}

satisfies
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Omega_{T})\leq T^{-\alpha}
. Moreover, for any
1\leq q\leq Q
, for
T
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\Big[\underset{k\in[K]}{\max}\hskip 1.42271pt\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}N^{k}([t-A,t[)^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{T}^{c}}\Big]\leq 2T^{-\alpha/2}.
